Despite these limitations, the paper makes a significant contribution to the field and has the potential to influence future research in time series generation and causal inference.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Poisoning Prompt-Guided Sampling in Video Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20851v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxin Cao, Wei Song, Jingling Xue, Jin Song Dong
- **Abstract**: Video Large Language Models (VideoLLMs) have emerged as powerful tools for understanding videos, supporting tasks such as summarization, captioning, and question answering. Their performance has been driven by advances in frame sampling, progressing from uniform-based to semantic-similarity-based and, most recently, prompt-guided strategies. While vulnerabilities have been identified in earlier sampling strategies, the safety of prompt-guided sampling remains unexplored. We close this gap by presenting PoisonVID, the first black-box poisoning attack that undermines prompt-guided sampling in VideoLLMs. PoisonVID compromises the underlying prompt-guided sampling mechanism through a closed-loop optimization strategy that iteratively optimizes a universal perturbation to suppress harmful frame relevance scores, guided by a depiction set constructed from paraphrased harmful descriptions leveraging a shadow VideoLLM and a lightweight language model, i.e., GPT-4o-mini. Comprehensively evaluated on three prompt-guided sampling strategies and across three advanced VideoLLMs, PoisonVID achieves 82% - 99% attack success rate, highlighting the importance of developing future advanced sampling strategies for VideoLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces POISONVID, a novel black-box poisoning attack designed to undermine prompt-guided sampling (PGS) in Video Large Language Models (VideoLLMs). The attack works by optimizing a universal perturbation applied to harmful video clips, making them less relevant to the prompt used for frame selection. This is achieved by minimizing the semantic similarity between the perturbed clip and a depiction set constructed from paraphrased harmful descriptions, generated using a shadow VideoLLM and a lightweight language model (GPT-40-mini).  The paper evaluates POISONVID on three PGS strategies (DKS, AKS, FRAG) across three VideoLLMs, demonstrating high attack success rates (82-99%). The work highlights the vulnerability of PGS to poisoning attacks and emphasizes the need for more robust sampling strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a significant contribution by identifying and exploiting a previously unaddressed vulnerability in PGS-based VideoLLMs. While prior work ([Cao et al., 2025]) explored poisoning attacks on VideoLLMs, it focused on weaknesses in uniform and semantic similarity sampling, which are becoming less prevalent due to the adoption of PGS.  The core novelty lies in the design of POISONVID to specifically target and disrupt the prompt-guided sampling mechanism. The black-box nature of the attack, using a shadow model and optimization strategy, is also a noteworthy aspect.

*   **Significance:** The findings are highly significant due to the increasing reliance on VideoLLMs in various applications. Demonstrating the vulnerability of PGS raises serious concerns about the reliability and safety of these models. The high attack success rates across different PGS strategies and VideoLLMs underscores the practical importance of addressing this vulnerability. The work motivates the development of more robust sampling strategies and defense mechanisms against poisoning attacks. The paper's emphasis on the *safety* aspect of VideoLLMs, in addition to performance, is valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a well-defined and practical attack framework.
    *   The use of a black-box approach enhances the practicality and relevance of the attack.
    *   The comprehensive evaluation across multiple PGS strategies and VideoLLMs strengthens the findings.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the key components of the attack.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the technical details.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper acknowledges the dependence on a shadow VideoLLM for generating depictions, which might limit the attack's effectiveness if the shadow model is significantly different from the target model. Although transferability experiments are presented, the analysis could be expanded.
    *   The use of GPT-40-mini, even for paraphrasing, might raise concerns about its potential limitations or biases. Exploring alternative lightweight language models or paraphrasing techniques could strengthen the robustness of the approach.
    *   The paper primarily focuses on a single type of poisoning attack (relevance score suppression). Exploring other potential attack vectors or combinations of attacks could broaden the threat landscape.
    * The paper does contain offensive content, which significantly reduces the potential positive impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to have a substantial influence on the field of VideoLLMs. It will likely stimulate research on more robust frame sampling strategies and defense mechanisms against poisoning attacks. It should also drive greater awareness of the safety implications of deploying VideoLLMs in real-world applications.

**Justification:**

POISONVID represents a significant advance in understanding the security vulnerabilities of VideoLLMs. The paper identifies and successfully exploits a previously unaddressed weakness in prompt-guided sampling. The comprehensive evaluation and insightful ablation studies strengthen the findings and contribute to a deeper understanding of the attack's effectiveness. The limitations, such as reliance on a shadow model and the limited exploration of attack vectors, slightly temper the score. While the black-box setting and focus on transferability enhance the practical relevance, further work is needed to explore the interplay of different types of poisoning attacks and defenses. Despite the presence of offensive content, the technical contribution of this work warrants high recognition.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### WeFT: Weighted Entropy-driven Fine-Tuning for dLLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20863v1)
- **Authors**: Guowei Xu, Wenxin Xu, Jiawang Zhao, Kaisheng Ma
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have recently shown strong potential in language modeling, offering faster generation compared to traditional autoregressive approaches. However, applying supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to diffusion models remains challenging, as they lack precise probability estimates at each denoising step. While the diffusion mechanism enables the model to reason over entire sequences, it also makes the generation process less predictable and often inconsistent. This highlights the importance of controlling key tokens that guide the direction of generation. To address this issue, we propose WeFT, a weighted SFT method for diffusion language models, where tokens are assigned different weights based on their entropy. Derived from diffusion theory, WeFT delivers substantial gains: training on s1K, s1K-1.1, and 3k samples from open-r1, it achieves relative improvements of 39%, 64%, and 83% over standard SFT on four widely used reasoning benchmarks (Sudoku, Countdown, GSM8K, and MATH-500). The code and models will be made publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "WEFT: WEIGHTED ENTROPY-DRIVEN FINE-TUNING FOR DLLMS":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WeFT (Weighted Entropy-Driven Fine-Tuning), a novel supervised fine-tuning (SFT) method tailored for diffusion language models (dLLMs). WeFT addresses the limitation of standard SFT, which treats all tokens equally during training. WeFT assigns different weights to tokens based on their predictive entropy during training, prioritizing tokens associated with higher uncertainty related to reasoning or planning. The method leverages two forward passes: one to estimate token-level entropy and another to apply weighted masking during the SFT loss computation.  The paper provides a theoretical justification rooted in diffusion process theory and demonstrates substantial empirical gains on reasoning benchmarks compared to standard SFT.  The results also show that models initialized with WeFT maintain their advantage during subsequent reinforcement learning stages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of weighting tokens based on predictive entropy during SFT is moderately novel. While token-level weighting in language models is not entirely new (as the paper acknowledges in the related work section), the application of *entropy* in the context of *diffusion* language models represents a worthwhile contribution. Existing weighting schemes typically focus on aspects like token frequency or utility derived from a reference model, not predictive uncertainty within the diffusion process. The theoretical justification, linking entropy weighting to the underlying diffusion dynamics, adds further weight to the novelty. The concept of prioritizing tokens related to planning/reasoning also provides useful insight.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of the work hinges on the demonstrated empirical improvements. Achieving substantial relative improvements (39-83%) across four reasoning benchmarks (Sudoku, Countdown, GSM8K, and MATH-500) is impressive, particularly given the relatively small datasets used for training (s1K, s1K-1.1, and 3k samples from open-r1).  The fact that WeFT-initialized models also perform better after subsequent RL training enhances the practical significance, as it suggests WeFT produces a better starting point for reinforcement learning fine-tuning. The runtime analysis is also important. Showing minimal increases in training time with the weighted fine-tuning helps justify the practical applications of this approach.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Strong empirical results on multiple benchmarks validate the effectiveness of WeFT.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides a theoretical foundation for the method, grounded in the principles of diffusion models.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Comprehensive ablation studies demonstrate the necessity of both the weighted SFT loss and the entropy-based weighting scheme.
    *   **Runtime Analysis:** The paper includes a runtime analysis, showing that WeFT introduces only minimal computational overhead.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Benchmark Selection:** While the benchmarks are established, the dataset size is relatively small. Experiments on much larger datasets would further solidify the findings.
    *   **Base Model:** The base model used, LLaDA-8B-Instruct, might not be representative of state-of-the-art pre-trained diffusion models. Showing improvements with more recent or widely used dLLMs would strengthen the impact.
    *   **Limited scope in the related work discussion**. The discussion on related works felt a bit limited.
    *   **Implementation Details:** The exact details of implementing the geometric distribution could be explained further.

*   **Potential Influence:**  WeFT has the potential to influence the development and fine-tuning of diffusion-based language models. By providing a more effective SFT method, WeFT can help improve the reasoning abilities of dLLMs. The insights into entropy and token importance can also inform the design of future training strategies.

**Overall:**

WeFT represents a valuable contribution to the field of diffusion language models. While the idea of token-level weighting is not entirely new, the combination of entropy-based weighting with a theoretically grounded diffusion SFT loss is a novel and effective approach. The empirical results are compelling, and the ablation studies provide further support for the design choices. While the work could benefit from validation on larger datasets and more modern pre-trained dLLMs, the current findings are significant and suggest that WeFT can improve reasoning performance and stability during fine-tuning.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a clear and well-supported method that demonstrates significant empirical gains on reasoning benchmarks within the field of diffusion language modeling. The novelty lies in the specific combination of entropy weighting and a theoretically grounded weighted SFT loss, addressing a clear limitation of standard SFT. Though it has strengths in its empirical validation, theoretical background and runtime analysis, it has a few limitations related to its benchmark selection and related work discussion. Overall, a **7.5** reflects the reasonable contribution this paper makes to the area and the high potential for follow-up work by this research group or other groups.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### StyleBench: Evaluating thinking styles in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20868v1)
- **Authors**: Junyu Guo, Shangding Gu, Ming Jin, Costas Spanos, Javad Lavaei
- **Abstract**: The effectiveness of Large Language Models (LLMs) is heavily influenced by the reasoning strategies, or styles of thought, employed in their prompts. However, the interplay between these reasoning styles, model architecture, and task type remains poorly understood. To address this, we introduce StyleBench, a comprehensive benchmark for systematically evaluating reasoning styles across diverse tasks and models. We assess five representative reasoning styles, including Chain of Thought (CoT), Tree of Thought (ToT), Algorithm of Thought (AoT), Sketch of Thought (SoT), and Chain-of-Draft (CoD) on five reasoning tasks, using 15 open-source models from major families (LLaMA, Qwen, Mistral, Gemma, GPT-OSS, Phi, and DeepSeek) ranging from 270M to 120B parameters. Our large-scale analysis reveals that no single style is universally optimal. We demonstrate that strategy efficacy is highly contingent on both model scale and task type: search-based methods (AoT, ToT) excel in open-ended problems but require large-scale models, while concise styles (SoT, CoD) achieve radical efficiency gains on well-defined tasks. Furthermore, we identify key behavioral patterns: smaller models frequently fail to follow output instructions and default to guessing, while reasoning robustness emerges as a function of scale. Our findings offer a crucial roadmap for selecting optimal reasoning strategies based on specific constraints, we open source the benchmark in https://github.com/JamesJunyuGuo/Style_Bench.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the StyleBench paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces StyleBench, a benchmark designed to systematically evaluate the impact of different reasoning strategies (or "thinking styles") on the performance of large language models (LLMs). StyleBench assesses five representative reasoning styles: Chain-of-Thought (CoT), Tree-of-Thought (ToT), Algorithm-of-Thought (AoT), Sketch-of-Thought (SoT), and Chain-of-Draft (CoD). These styles are evaluated across five diverse reasoning tasks using 15 open-source LLMs from various model families and parameter scales (ranging from 270M to 120B). The authors' analysis reveals that no single reasoning style is universally optimal, and strategy efficacy heavily depends on both model scale and task type. For instance, search-based methods (AoT, ToT) excel in open-ended problems when applied to large-scale models, while concise styles (SoT, CoD) achieve efficiency gains on well-defined tasks. The study also identifies behavioral patterns, such as smaller models often resorting to guessing when unable to follow instructions, and the correlation between model scale and reasoning robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** StyleBench offers a valuable and systematic evaluation framework. By examining a range of reasoning styles, tasks, and model sizes, it provides a much-needed holistic view.
    *   **Extensive Model Coverage:** The inclusion of 15 open-source LLMs across different model families and scales enhances the generalizability and robustness of the findings. This allows the paper to uncover scaling trends across families of models.
    *   **Model-Style and Task-Style Interaction Analysis:**  The paper identifies critical relationships between reasoning styles, model architecture, and task types, moving beyond a simplistic "one-size-fits-all" approach to prompting. The identification of specific task-style affinities is particularly helpful.
    *   **Focus on Efficiency:** The inclusion of computational efficiency as a key evaluation metric is highly relevant for real-world applications.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The qualitative analysis of failure modes helps to provide richer insight than just quantitative metrics. It helps explain observed behavior.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited to Defined Reasoning Styles:**  The benchmark focuses on a pre-selected set of reasoning styles.  While these are representative, the framework could potentially be extended to incorporate more novel or adaptive strategies as the field evolves.
    *   **Open-Source LLMs Only:**  While including open-source models is a strength in terms of accessibility and reproducibility, the absence of evaluations on proprietary models like GPT-4 or PaLM limits the scope of the analysis and its conclusions, since the families often have architectural differences.
    *   **Static Prompts:**  The analysis focuses on static prompts for each style. Future benchmarks might incorporate adaptive prompt generation or meta-reasoning to dynamically select prompts, mirroring more realistic scenarios.
    *   **Supervised Fine-Tuning Limitations:** The attempt to automatically select reasoning styles via supervised fine-tuning is interesting but reveals limitations of the current SFT techniques. It would benefit by exploring more sophisticated meta-learning or reinforcement learning approaches.
    *   **Task Selection**: It would be beneficial to have a more diverse set of tasks. For example, there are no tasks involving the models ability to generate code, an area in which CoT has shown promising results.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The work provides a more comprehensive understanding of the interplay between reasoning styles, model scale, and task types than prior works.
    *   The results provide empirical guidance for selecting optimal reasoning strategies based on constraints like model size and task complexity.
    *   By providing clear code and benchmarks, it makes it accessible for other researchers to study or improve.
    *   Identifies scaling laws around the performance of a variety of styles, giving insight into how model scale interacts with reasoning style.

**Justification for Score:**

StyleBench represents a valuable and significant contribution to the LLM research community. It goes beyond simple performance comparisons and offers a more nuanced understanding of how reasoning strategies affect model behavior. The extensive model coverage and the focus on efficiency make it a highly practical resource. While some limitations exist, particularly regarding proprietary models and prompting techniques, the benchmark significantly advances the field. Thus, a score of 7.5 out of 10 is warranted.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SCRA-VQA: Summarized Caption-Rerank for Augmented Large Language Models in Visual Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20871v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Zhang, Jiaqing Lin, Miao Zhang, Kui Xiao, Xiaoju Hou, Yue Zhao, Zhifei Li
- **Abstract**: Acquiring high-quality knowledge is a central focus in Knowledge-Based Visual Question Answering (KB-VQA). Recent methods use large language models (LLMs) as knowledge engines for answering. These methods generally employ image captions as visual text descriptions to assist LLMs in interpreting images. However, the captions frequently include excessive noise irrelevant to the question, and LLMs generally do not comprehend VQA tasks, limiting their reasoning capabilities. To address this issue, we propose the Summarized Caption-Rerank Augmented VQA (SCRA-VQA), which employs a pre-trained visual language model to convert images into captions. Moreover, SCRA-VQA generates contextual examples for the captions while simultaneously summarizing and reordering them to exclude unrelated information. The caption-rerank process enables LLMs to understand the image information and questions better, thus enhancing the model's reasoning ability and task adaptability without expensive end-to-end training. Based on an LLM with 6.7B parameters, SCRA-VQA performs excellently on two challenging knowledge-based VQA datasets: OK-VQA and A-OKVQA, achieving accuracies of 38.8% and 34.6%. Our code is available at https://github.com/HubuKG/SCRA-VQA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SCRA-VQA (Summarized Caption-Rerank Augmented VQA), a novel approach for knowledge-based visual question answering (KB-VQA) that leverages large language models (LLMs). The core idea is to improve the image understanding of LLMs by processing captions generated from images in a way that makes them more relevant to the posed question. This involves:

1.  Generating image captions using a pre-trained vision-language model (BLIP).
2.  Creating contextual examples by generating similar question-answer pairs from the captions.
3.  Summarizing and re-ranking the captions to remove irrelevant information and highlight important content.

SCRA-VQA uses a frozen LLM (OPT in the main experiments) with a carefully designed prompt that includes the re-ranked captions, summaries, and contextual examples.  The authors demonstrate strong performance on the OK-VQA and A-OKVQA datasets, showing that SCRA-VQA enhances the reasoning ability and task adaptability of LLMs without requiring expensive end-to-end training.  A key aspect is the method’s lightweight deployment. Ablation studies analyze the impact of each module.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper proposes a reasonable pipeline and combines known components (e.g. BLIP, LLMs) but its novelty hinges on the specific combination and the caption processing steps. Summarization and re-ranking of captions is a valuable idea, especially for KB-VQA tasks where noisy or irrelevant information can significantly hinder the LLM's reasoning. The use of generated question/answer pairs as a form of in-context learning also builds on existing approaches. However, the individual components are not groundbreaking on their own. The novelty primarily resides in the **integration and careful tuning** of these elements.  More insight into how the contextual examples are generated and how the summaries are created would strengthen the claim to novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its ability to improve the performance of LLMs on KB-VQA tasks with a relatively simple and efficient approach. The ability to leverage frozen LLMs without extensive fine-tuning is a major advantage, making the method more accessible and practical. The reported improvements over baselines, particularly on the more challenging A-OKVQA dataset, suggest that the proposed approach is effective in enhancing the LLM's reasoning abilities. The method potentially provides better visual data integration for LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is well-motivated, addressing the issue of noisy and irrelevant information in image captions for KB-VQA.
    *   The modular design allows for easy adaptation and experimentation with different components (e.g., different LLMs, different captioning models).
    *   The experiments are thorough, including ablation studies and hyperparameter sensitivity analysis.
    *   The code is publicly available, facilitating reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper relies heavily on existing pre-trained models (BLIP, OPT, Gemma2b), and the improvements might be sensitive to the specific choice of these models. The reliance on proprietary models (such as GPT-3 for related research) may also be a limiting factor.
    *   More detailed analysis on qualitative failure cases might reveal limitations.
    *   While the authors claim lightweight deployment, no information of the inference computational time or resources is shown to support this claim.
    *   The novelty is incremental, building on existing techniques for captioning, re-ranking, and prompting LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:** SCRA-VQA has the potential to influence future research on KB-VQA by providing a simple and effective approach for leveraging LLMs. The method's modular design and open-source code could encourage further exploration of different caption processing techniques and LLM prompting strategies. It is also important since it demonstrates that significant improvements can be achieved with relatively small LLMs (6.7B parameters) compared to the massive models often used in VQA tasks.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of KB-VQA by presenting a practical and effective method for improving the image understanding of LLMs. While the individual components are not entirely novel, their integration and careful tuning demonstrate ingenuity. The improvements over existing baselines and the thorough experimental analysis support the method's effectiveness. However, the incremental nature of the novelty and the reliance on pre-trained models limit the score.  More in-depth analysis and investigation of failure cases, would potentially lead to a higher score. Similarly, an analysis on the memory load of the summarization/reranking process would improve the work’s appeal.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Nuclear Diffusion Models for Low-Rank Background Suppression in Videos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20886v1)
- **Authors**: Tristan S. W. Stevens, Oisín Nolan, Jean-Luc Robert, Ruud J. G. van Sloun
- **Abstract**: Video sequences often contain structured noise and background artifacts that obscure dynamic content, posing challenges for accurate analysis and restoration. Robust principal component methods address this by decomposing data into low-rank and sparse components. Still, the sparsity assumption often fails to capture the rich variability present in real video data. To overcome this limitation, a hybrid framework that integrates low-rank temporal modeling with diffusion posterior sampling is proposed. The proposed method, Nuclear Diffusion, is evaluated on a real-world medical imaging problem, namely cardiac ultrasound dehazing, and demonstrates improved dehazing performance compared to traditional RPCA concerning contrast enhancement (gCNR) and signal preservation (KS statistic). These results highlight the potential of combining model-based temporal models with deep generative priors for high-fidelity video restoration.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a hybrid framework, called "Nuclear Diffusion," for low-rank background suppression in videos.  It combines Robust Principal Component Analysis (RPCA) with Diffusion Posterior Sampling (DPS) using deep generative models. The core idea is to replace the traditional sparsity assumption in RPCA with a learned diffusion prior that better captures complex foreground dynamics, while retaining a nuclear norm penalty for low-rank background modeling.  The method is evaluated on cardiac ultrasound dehazing, where it demonstrates improved contrast enhancement and tissue preservation compared to standard RPCA. The authors argue that combining model-based temporal models with deep generative priors can lead to higher-fidelity video restoration.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the integration of DPS and low-rank temporal modeling, specifically applied to the RPCA framework. While RPCA and diffusion models are independently established techniques, their combination in this specific way, especially the replacement of the sparsity prior with a learned diffusion prior, is a non-trivial contribution. The application to cardiac ultrasound dehazing provides a practical use case.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a known limitation of traditional RPCA: its reliance on a simple sparsity assumption. By leveraging the expressive power of diffusion models, the proposed method can potentially handle more complex foreground dynamics, leading to improved performance in scenarios where the foreground is not truly sparse. The improved results on cardiac ultrasound data suggest a potential clinical impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology combining RPCA and DPS.
    *   Demonstrated improvements in both qualitative and quantitative results compared to RPCA.
    *   Application to a relevant and challenging real-world problem (cardiac ultrasound dehazing).
    *   Uses modern techniques like SeqDiff for acceleration which improves adoption potential.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on pretrained 2D diffusion models, while simplifying implementation, might limit the ability to capture intricate temporal correlations within the video sequence. Ideally a 3D model, or something more temporal aware may show better results.
    *   The evaluation is limited to a single application (cardiac ultrasound). More diverse datasets would strengthen the generalizability claim.
    *   While the quantitative metrics (gCNR and KS) provide some insight, the absence of ground truth makes it difficult to definitively assess the accuracy of the restored images.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the limitations and potential failure modes of the proposed method.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by demonstrating the benefits of combining classical signal processing techniques with deep generative models. It opens up avenues for exploring other hybrid approaches that leverage the strengths of both model-based and data-driven methods. The specific application to cardiac ultrasound could also inspire further research in medical image restoration.

*   **Justification:** The paper provides a solid contribution, even if incremental. It addresses a known problem, proposes a reasonably novel solution, and demonstrates its effectiveness through experiments. It showcases the value of incorporating learned priors into a classical method (RPCA), which is a valuable concept. However, the evaluation could be more extensive, and the limitations should be discussed more thoroughly.

**Score: 7**

The paper presents a well-motivated and executed integration of RPCA and diffusion models, addressing a limitation of RPCA with demonstrable results in a relevant application. While the evaluation could be broader and the limitations more explicitly addressed, the work showcases the potential of hybrid approaches and contributes meaningfully to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### AIBA: Attention-based Instrument Band Alignment for Text-to-Audio Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20891v1)
- **Authors**: Junyoung Koh, Soo Yong Kim, Gyu Hyeong Choi, Yongwon Choi
- **Abstract**: We present AIBA (Attention-In-Band Alignment), a lightweight, training-free pipeline to quantify where text-to-audio diffusion models attend on the time-frequency (T-F) plane. AIBA (i) hooks cross-attention at inference to record attention probabilities without modifying weights; (ii) projects them to fixed-size mel grids that are directly comparable to audio energy; and (iii) scores agreement with instrument-band ground truth via interpretable metrics (T-F IoU/AP, frequency-profile correlation, and a pointing game). On Slakh2100 with an AudioLDM2 backbone, AIBA reveals consistent instrument-dependent trends (e.g., bass favoring low bands) and achieves high precision with moderate recall.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AIBA: Attention-based Instrument Band Alignment for Text-to-Audio Diffusion":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AIBA (Attention-In-Band Alignment), a training-free pipeline for analyzing where text-to-audio diffusion models focus their attention in the time-frequency (T-F) domain. AIBA works by: (i) extracting attention probabilities from cross-attention mechanisms in the diffusion model; (ii) mapping these probabilities to a fixed-size mel-grid representation; and (iii) evaluating the alignment between the resulting attention map and instrument-specific frequency band templates using interpretable metrics.  The authors demonstrate AIBA on AudioLDM2 and Slakh2100, showing that the approach can reveal consistent, instrument-dependent attention patterns. The code is made available to promote reproducibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of visualizing and quantifying attention in text-to-audio diffusion models is valuable.  While attention visualization is common in vision, its adoption in audio, particularly with instrument-specific alignment, is relatively less explored quantitatively. AIBA's training-free nature is also a plus. The approach is presented as relatively model-agnostic, although the results are only showcased on AudioLDM2. The metrics are straightforward, which aids interpretability.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the interpretability of text-to-audio diffusion models. Understanding how these models attend to different frequency bands for various instruments helps diagnose potential issues, guide model development, and potentially improve controllable audio generation. Demonstrating the pipeline works with real-world music and providing the code for reuse gives practical value to the community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The steps of AIBA are explained clearly and precisely.
    *   **Reproducibility:**  The authors explicitly address reproducibility by releasing their code.
    *   **Practical Evaluation:**  Testing on Slakh2100 provides a realistic test case and useful statistics.
    *   **Model Agnostic (Partially):** It aims to work with diffusers but failed to work out for token concatenation diffusion.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Testing:** The analysis is mostly restricted to AudioLDM2.  Claiming model-agnosticism is slightly overblown given DiT's inability.
    *   **Relatively Simple Metrics:**  The metrics used (IoU, correlation, pointing game) are standard.  While interpretable, they might not capture the full nuances of attention alignment.
    *   **Lower Recall:** The paper acknowledges a precision-recall gap. Improving attention aggregation to cover wider ground-truth bands could improve performance, but the paper doesn't address it in depth.
    *   **Limited Dataset for Evaluation:** Only Slakh2100 dataset is used for evaluating performance, leaving question about how well would AIBA do on other kinds of dataset.
    *   **Heuristic Cross/Self Tagging** is mentioned, which is somewhat weak spot.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a valuable tool for the audio community to understand the inner workings of text-to-audio models. It could inspire further research into attention mechanisms, controllable generation, and more sophisticated methods for aligning model attention with musical structures.

**Rationale for the Score:**

The paper provides a worthwhile contribution to the field of text-to-audio generation by offering a practical tool for interpreting model attention. While the novelty and significance are solid, the limitations in model testing, the relative simplicity of the metrics, and the precision-recall gap prevent it from achieving a higher score. The demonstrated real-world evaluation and code release are strong points, however, these alone are not sufficient to push the novelty beyond the core innovation itself.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deterministic Discrete Denoising
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20896v1)
- **Authors**: Hideyuki Suzuki, Hiroshi Yamashita
- **Abstract**: We propose a deterministic denoising algorithm for discrete-state diffusion models based on Markov chains. The generative reverse process is derandomized by introducing a variant of the herding algorithm with weakly chaotic dynamics, which induces deterministic discrete state transitions. Our approach is a direct replacement for the stochastic denoising process, requiring neither retraining nor continuous state embeddings. We demonstrate consistent improvements in both efficiency and sample quality on text and image generation tasks. Thus, this simple derandomization approach is expected to enhance the significance of discrete diffusion in generative modeling. Furthermore, our results reveal that deterministic reverse processes, well established in continuous diffusion, can also be effective in discrete state spaces.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a deterministic denoising algorithm for discrete-state diffusion models.  Instead of using stochastic sampling in the reverse denoising process, the authors propose a derandomized approach based on a variant of the herding algorithm. This algorithm associates continuous weight variables with each discrete variable, leading to deterministic updates of both. The method is presented as a drop-in replacement for stochastic denoising, requiring no retraining or continuous embeddings. Experiments on text and image generation demonstrate improvements in efficiency and sample quality compared to stochastic denoising. The paper emphasizes that deterministic reverse processes, which are well-established for continuous diffusion models, can also be effective in discrete spaces.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of applying a herding-based algorithm to derandomize the reverse process in discrete diffusion models is reasonably novel. While herding algorithms have been used in other contexts, their direct application in this setting, particularly as a drop-in replacement without requiring retraining, is a distinct contribution. The connection drawn to continuous diffusion's deterministic denoising techniques is also insightful.  However, the algorithm itself is essentially a tweaked version of existing herding techniques. The novelty lies more in the application than in a radically new theoretical concept. The "delayed-switching mechanism" adds a layer of refinement, but its impact may be limited. The exploration of different delta values adds to the novelty.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in discrete diffusion models: the potential inefficiency and limitations of stochastic sampling in the reverse process. By providing a deterministic alternative, the authors offer a way to potentially improve both the speed and quality of sample generation. The experimental results, showing improvements in perplexity and FID/IS scores, support this claim. This can encourage the adoption and further exploration of discrete diffusion models. The fact that it's a drop-in replacement makes the barrier to adoption very low, which can significantly improve the generative process. The observation that deterministic methods can also be effective in discrete spaces provides a useful perspective for future research.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed algorithm and its motivation.
    * **Drop-in Replacement:** The emphasis on the method being a drop-in replacement without retraining is a significant advantage. This practical aspect makes the approach more appealing and easier to adopt.
    * **Experimental Validation:** The experiments on text and image generation provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed algorithm.  The comparison to stochastic denoising and the exploration of different parameter settings are well-executed. Demonstrating the results on LM1B is especially valuable. The improved perplexity and image scores are encouraging.
    * **Connections to Existing Work:** The paper effectively relates the proposed algorithm to existing work on herding algorithms, continuous diffusion models, and the ΔΣ-modulator.  This context helps to understand the algorithm's theoretical underpinnings and potential connections to other areas.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Theoretical Analysis:**  While the paper provides some intuition for why the herding algorithm works in this context, a deeper theoretical analysis of its convergence properties and stability would strengthen the paper.  Specifically, a more rigorous explanation of how the delayed switching mitigates chattering and affects the overall dynamics would be beneficial.
    * **Parameter Sensitivity:** The delayed switching mechanism introduces a new parameter (δ) that needs to be tuned. The paper provides some guidance on how to choose δ, but more detailed analysis of its sensitivity and how it interacts with other parameters would be valuable.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** While the results show improvements, they are not revolutionary. The improvements aren't groundbreaking.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research on discrete diffusion models by encouraging the exploration of deterministic denoising techniques.  The simple and effective nature of the proposed algorithm could make it a popular starting point for future research. The drop-in replacement feature is important for widespread usage and adoption.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a worthwhile contribution to the field of discrete diffusion models. The idea of derandomizing the reverse process using a herding-based algorithm is interesting and has been experimentally validated. While there are some weaknesses in terms of theoretical analysis, the practical benefits of the proposed algorithm and its potential to improve both efficiency and sample quality make it a valuable addition to the literature.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning to Summarize by Learning to Quiz: Adversarial Agentic Collaboration for Long Document Summarization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20900v1)
- **Authors**: Weixuan Wang, Minghao Wu, Barry Haddow, Alexandra Birch
- **Abstract**: Long document summarization remains a significant challenge for current large language models (LLMs), as existing approaches commonly struggle with information loss, factual inconsistencies, and coherence issues when processing excessively long documents. We propose SummQ, a novel adversarial multi-agent framework that addresses these limitations through collaborative intelligence between specialized agents operating in two complementary domains: summarization and quizzing. Our approach employs summary generators and reviewers that work collaboratively to create and evaluate comprehensive summaries, while quiz generators and reviewers create comprehension questions that serve as continuous quality checks for the summarization process. This adversarial dynamic, enhanced by an examinee agent that validates whether the generated summary contains the information needed to answer the quiz questions, enables iterative refinement through multifaceted feedback mechanisms. We evaluate SummQ on three widely used long document summarization benchmarks. Experimental results demonstrate that our framework significantly outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods across ROUGE and BERTScore metrics, as well as in LLM-as-a-Judge and human evaluations. Our comprehensive analyses reveal the effectiveness of the multi-agent collaboration dynamics, the influence of different agent configurations, and the impact of the quizzing mechanism. This work establishes a new approach for long document summarization that uses adversarial agentic collaboration to improve summarization quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SUMMQ, a novel adversarial multi-agent framework designed to improve long document summarization.  It addresses limitations of current large language models (LLMs) such as information loss, factual inconsistencies, and coherence issues by using collaborative intelligence between specialized agents in summarization and quizzing.  The framework consists of summary generators and reviewers, quiz generators and reviewers, and an examinee agent. The quiz generation and review process serves as a continuous quality check on the summarization process. The examinee agent validates if the generated summary contains the information needed to answer the quiz. The method is evaluated on three long document summarization benchmarks and demonstrated significant improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods across ROUGE, BERTScore, LLM-as-a-Judge, and human evaluations. The paper includes in-depth analyses of the multi-agent collaboration dynamics, the influence of different agent configurations, and the impact of the quizzing mechanism.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper's novelty lies in its innovative integration of summarization and quizzing within a multi-agent adversarial framework. The use of quizzes as a means of assessing and improving summary quality is a clever approach.  The framework elegantly addresses several shortcomings of existing long document summarization techniques that struggle with long-range dependencies, factual accuracy, and coherence. The extensive empirical evaluations across several benchmarks and evaluation metrics provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of SUMMQ. The ablation studies and parameter sensitivity analysis are valuable in understanding the contribution of each component of the framework.

However, the paper has some limitations.

*   **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't explicitly address the computational cost associated with running multiple agents, including LLMs, and their iterative interactions. The efficiency of the framework, especially when scaling to larger documents or a greater number of agents, could be a concern. This should be addressed.

*   **Agent Backbone Dependency:** The reliance on GPT-4 and other powerful LLMs as the agent backbone might limit the accessibility and reproducibility of the results.  While the paper explores different LLM combinations, it doesn't sufficiently investigate the performance with more resource-constrained models. A more detailed analysis of the sensitivity to the choice of underlying LLM would be valuable.

*   **Examinee Agent limitations:** The examinee agent answers questions in a limited way which removes its own linguistic flexibility and ability to synthesize information. Its approach to answering questions, while potentially making it an easier agent to implement, loses the ability to be a true test.

*   **Generality Concerns:** The performance gains may be highly dependent on the specific prompts used for the agents, which might require extensive tuning for different types of documents or datasets. The paper could be strengthened by providing a more robust discussion of prompt engineering and transferability.

*   **Lack of Comparison to Chunking and Windowing Methods:** While the paper cites long-context methods, there is little comparison in the work to chunking and windowing methods that handle the same problem of long-range context. While agents are more flexible, direct comparisons would highlight strengths and weaknesses.

**Significance:**

Despite the limitations, SUMMQ represents a significant advance in long document summarization. The combination of summarization and quizzing offers a promising direction for improving summary quality.  The modular design of the framework allows for easy adaptation and integration with other summarization techniques. The work could stimulate further research into adversarial multi-agent systems and the use of quizzes for assessing and improving text generation tasks.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The score reflects the paper's novelty and strong empirical results, balanced against the limitations discussed above. The paper presents a well-designed framework with clear benefits in terms of summarization quality. However, the computational cost, LLM backbone dependency, and potential prompt sensitivity require further investigation. While the gains are promising, the lack of direct comparison to chunking and windowing lowers the overall score. If the work addressed more concerns about deployment cost/complexity as well as generality, this would have a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MemLens: Uncovering Memorization in LLMs with Activation Trajectories
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20909v1)
- **Authors**: Zirui He, Haiyan Zhao, Ali Payani, Mengnan du
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are commonly evaluated on challenging benchmarks such as AIME and Math500, which are susceptible to contamination and risk of being memorized. Existing detection methods, which primarily rely on surface-level lexical overlap and perplexity, demonstrate low generalization and degrade significantly when encountering implicitly contaminated data. In this paper, we propose MemLens (An Activation Lens for Memorization Detection) to detect memorization by analyzing the probability trajectories of numeric tokens during generation. Our method reveals that contaminated samples exhibit ``shortcut'' behaviors, locking onto an answer with high confidence in the model's early layers, whereas clean samples show more gradual evidence accumulation across the model's full depth. We observe that contaminated and clean samples exhibit distinct and well-separated reasoning trajectories. To further validate this, we inject carefully designed samples into the model through LoRA fine-tuning and observe the same trajectory patterns as in naturally contaminated data. These results provide strong evidence that MemLens captures genuine signals of memorization rather than spurious correlations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MemLens, a novel approach to detecting memorization in large language models (LLMs) caused by data contamination.  Unlike existing methods that rely on surface-level lexical overlap or perplexity, MemLens analyzes the probability trajectories of numeric tokens during generation.  The method hypothesizes that memorized samples exhibit "shortcut" reasoning, locking onto an answer early in the model's processing, while clean samples show more gradual evidence accumulation. The authors validate this hypothesis by showing distinct trajectory patterns between contaminated and clean samples, confirming the findings with controlled experiments of injecting contaminated data through LoRA fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach.  Analyzing activation trajectories for memorization detection is a significant departure from existing methods that focus on input-output relationships or distributional properties. The key insight of identifying "shortcut" reasoning pathways based on these trajectories is well-articulated. The use of a CNN-based discriminator trained on these trajectories further improves the approach. The LoRA injection technique to validate causal relationships between memorization and the observed trajectories adds substantial value.
* **Significance:**  The significance lies in the limitations of current contamination detection methods. Prior methods struggle with paraphrased or structurally altered problems. By using activation trajectories, MemLens shows greater robustness to these variations. The method demonstrates improved detection rates compared to completion-based recall tests, perplexity-based approaches and output distributional analysis methods.  The discovery and analysis of internal model representations related to memorization is also valuable for the broader understanding of how LLMs work.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Methodology:**  The methodology is rigorous, incorporating controlled experiments, ablation studies, and causal validation. The problem formulation, feature construction, and discriminator design are well-described.
    *   **Improved Robustness:**  The experimental results clearly show that MemLens is more robust to input variations (rephrasing, perturbation, translation) compared to existing methods.
    *   **Causal Validation:**  The LoRA injection experiments provide strong causal evidence that MemLens captures genuine memorization signals, not just spurious correlations.
    *   **Insights into Memorization Mechanisms:**  The analysis of activation trajectories provides valuable insights into how memorization manifests within the LLM's internal representations (e.g., early dominance of a single digit channel).

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Task Domain:** The focus is primarily on numerical reasoning tasks and relies on analyzing numeric token probabilities. This might limit the generalizability of the method to other types of tasks (e.g., text generation, summarization) where the relevant tokens are not easily identifiable.
    *   **Computational Cost:**  Extracting and processing activation trajectories from multiple layers can be computationally intensive, potentially limiting the scalability of MemLens to extremely large models or datasets. Although, the paper trains a discriminator with just the top 30 layers and that is still enough to achieve reasonable results
    *   **Need for Extensive Dataset**: Requires the creation of both original and contaminated sets to be properly tuned.
* **Potential Influence:**  MemLens has the potential to significantly influence research on data contamination and evaluation of LLMs.  It provides a new direction for detection methods and helps better understand how memorization arises in these models. This could lead to more robust evaluation metrics, improved training strategies, and mitigation techniques to address memorization.

**Overall:**

MemLens is a well-executed and significant contribution to the field of LLM evaluation. Its novel approach, improved robustness, causal validation, and insights into memorization mechanisms justify a strong positive evaluation. The main weakness is the task-specific nature of the token analysis, but the fundamental idea of analyzing activation trajectories to capture memorization is transferable.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### SwinMamba: A hybrid local-global mamba framework for enhancing semantic segmentation of remotely sensed images
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20918v1)
- **Authors**: Qinfeng Zhu, Han Li, Liang He, Lei Fan
- **Abstract**: Semantic segmentation of remote sensing imagery is a fundamental task in computer vision, supporting a wide range of applications such as land use classification, urban planning, and environmental monitoring. However, this task is often challenged by the high spatial resolution, complex scene structures, and diverse object scales present in remote sensing data. To address these challenges, various deep learning architectures have been proposed, including convolutional neural networks, Vision Transformers, and the recently introduced Vision Mamba. Vision Mamba features a global receptive field and low computational complexity, demonstrating both efficiency and effectiveness in image segmentation. However, its reliance on global scanning tends to overlook critical local features, such as textures and edges, which are essential for achieving accurate segmentation in remote sensing contexts. To tackle this limitation, we propose SwinMamba, a novel framework inspired by the Swin Transformer. SwinMamba integrates localized Mamba-style scanning within shifted windows with a global receptive field, to enhance the model's perception of both local and global features. Specifically, the first two stages of SwinMamba perform local scanning to capture fine-grained details, while its subsequent two stages leverage global scanning to fuse broader contextual information. In our model, the use of overlapping shifted windows enhances inter-region information exchange, facilitating more robust feature integration across the entire image. Extensive experiments on the LoveDA and ISPRS Potsdam datasets demonstrate that SwinMamba outperforms state-of-the-art methods, underscoring its effectiveness and potential as a superior solution for semantic segmentation of remotely sensed imagery.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SwinMamba, a novel hybrid framework for semantic segmentation of remotely sensed images. It addresses the limitations of existing Vision Mamba models, which primarily rely on global scanning and may overlook critical local features like textures and edges. SwinMamba integrates localized Mamba-style scanning within shifted windows, inspired by the Swin Transformer, to capture fine-grained details in the early stages. It transitions to global scanning in later stages to incorporate broader contextual information. Experiments on the LoveDA and ISPRS Potsdam datasets demonstrate that SwinMamba outperforms state-of-the-art methods, showcasing its effectiveness and potential.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the fusion of local and global scanning strategies within the Mamba architecture. While Mamba itself is a relatively recent advance, its application to remote sensing and, more specifically, its adaptation for localized feature extraction are potentially significant. The use of shifted windows to facilitate information exchange between local regions is directly inspired by Swin Transformer but is an original application within the Mamba framework. Prior work focused heavily on optimized global scanning paths for Mamba; this paper moves in a different direction, considering local-global fusion. However, its roots heavily depend on two architectures that are well established, Swin Transformer and Vision Mamba.

*   **Significance:** Semantic segmentation of remotely sensed images is crucial for various applications. The challenges associated with high spatial resolution, complex scenes, and diverse object scales make this task difficult. Existing Mamba-based approaches have shown promise, but their limitations in capturing local details hinder their performance. SwinMamba aims to overcome this by explicitly addressing the need for both local and global contextual awareness. Demonstrating improved performance on standard benchmarks like LoveDA and ISPRS Potsdam suggests that SwinMamba is a valuable contribution to the field. The paper shows that the introduced network leads to improved accuracy compared to state-of-the-art networks. Future application of the network will likely follow this publication.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing Mamba-based approaches for remote sensing image segmentation.
    *   The proposed SwinMamba framework is well-motivated and conceptually sound.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of SwinMamba on two widely used datasets.
    *   The ablation study provides insights into the importance of the hybrid scanning strategy.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty, while present, builds directly on well-established architectures (Swin Transformer and Vision Mamba).
    *   While performance gains are shown, the gains over state-of-the-art Mamba are relatively marginal and would be more convincing with a more extensive benchmark set or in real world setting.
    *   The paper does not address the computational complexity added by the shifting windows when compared with vanilla vision mamba. This may be of critical importance especially in remote sensing applications where large, high-resolution images are often used.
    *   The paper doesn't deeply explore the limitations of fixed window sizes. They point out the potential of future work around adaptive window sizes, but more thorough discussion is needed.

*   **Potential Influence:** SwinMamba could influence future research by:
    *   Encouraging the development of hybrid architectures that combine local and global feature extraction for remote sensing image segmentation.
    *   Inspiring further exploration of alternative scanning strategies for Mamba-based models.
    *   Providing a strong baseline for future comparisons.
    *   Expanding the application to multimodal data fusion or adaptive windows.

Overall, SwinMamba presents a valuable contribution to the field of remote sensing image segmentation by addressing a specific limitation of existing Mamba-based approaches. The use of localized scanning within shifted windows is a novel adaptation, and the experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework. However, the paper needs stronger justification for the computational expense, discussion on the limitation of fixed window sizes, and a better demonstration that the accuracy gains are statistically significant.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### RLCracker: Exposing the Vulnerability of LLM Watermarks with Adaptive RL Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20924v1)
- **Authors**: Hanbo Huang, Yiran Zhang, Hao Zheng, Xuan Gong, Yihan Li, Lin Liu, Shiyu Liang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) watermarking has shown promise in detecting AI-generated content and mitigating misuse, with prior work claiming robustness against paraphrasing and text editing. In this paper, we argue that existing evaluations are not sufficiently adversarial, obscuring critical vulnerabilities and overstating the security. To address this, we introduce adaptive robustness radius, a formal metric that quantifies watermark resilience against adaptive adversaries. We theoretically prove that optimizing the attack context and model parameters can substantially reduce this radius, making watermarks highly susceptible to paraphrase attacks. Leveraging this insight, we propose RLCracker, a reinforcement learning (RL)-based adaptive attack that erases watermarks while preserving semantic fidelity. RLCracker requires only limited watermarked examples and zero access to the detector. Despite weak supervision, it empowers a 3B model to achieve 98.5% removal success and an average 0.92 P-SP score on 1,500-token Unigram-marked texts after training on only 100 short samples. This performance dramatically exceeds 6.75% by GPT-4o and generalizes across five model sizes over ten watermarking schemes. Our results confirm that adaptive attacks are broadly effective and pose a fundamental threat to current watermarking defenses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "RLCRACKER: EXPOSING THE VULNERABILITY OF LLM WATERMARKS WITH ADAPTIVE RL ATTACKS" tackles the problem of assessing the robustness of watermarking schemes for Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors argue that existing evaluation methods are not sufficiently adversarial and can overestimate the security of watermarks. To address this, they introduce the concept of "adaptive robustness radius," a formal metric to quantify watermark resilience against adaptive adversaries. They theoretically and empirically demonstrate that optimizing the attack context and model parameters can significantly reduce this radius, making watermarks vulnerable to paraphrase attacks. They then propose RLCracker, a reinforcement learning (RL)-based adaptive attack that removes watermarks while preserving semantic fidelity. RLCracker requires limited watermarked examples and no access to the detector. The authors show that RLCracker dramatically outperforms existing attacks and generalizes across different model sizes and watermarking schemes, exposing critical vulnerabilities in current defenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers several novel contributions:
    *   **Adaptive Robustness Radius:** This is a new metric to rigorously quantify watermark robustness against adaptive attacks, going beyond simple average-case evaluations. This metric is the strongest part of the paper.
    *   **RLCracker:**  An efficient RL-based attack that removes watermarks without detector access is a significant practical contribution. The attack requires only a small dataset and no access to the watermark detector, which simulates a very realistic threat model.
    *   **Comprehensive Empirical Evaluation:** The experiments are extensive, covering multiple model sizes, watermarking schemes, and datasets. This provides strong evidence supporting the theoretical claims and demonstrates the practical effectiveness of RLCracker.
    *   **Underexplored Factors:** Highlighting the importance of system prompts and model reasoning ability in attack success is novel and provides valuable insights.

*   **Significance:**  The paper has significant implications for the field of LLM security and watermarking:
    *   **Realistically Assesses Watermark Vulnerability:** The introduction of the adaptive robustness radius and the use of an RL-based attack offer a more rigorous and realistic evaluation methodology than prior work.
    *   **Highlights Limitations of Existing Defenses:**  The empirical results clearly demonstrate that existing watermarking schemes are more vulnerable than previously thought, especially against adaptive adversaries.
    *   **Guides Development of More Robust Defenses:** By identifying key vulnerabilities and adversarial strategies, the paper can guide the development of more robust watermarking schemes.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Theoretical Foundation:** The paper is grounded in a well-defined theoretical framework based on information theory and distributional robustness.
    *   **Practical Implementation:** The RLCracker attack is well-described and easy to implement, making it accessible to other researchers.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The authors present a comprehensive set of experiments that validate their theoretical claims and demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach.
    *   **Clear Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, despite the technical complexity of the topic.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost of RL:** The RL-based attack, while efficient in terms of data requirements, may have a non-trivial computational cost for training the attacker policy. This may limit its accessibility for some researchers. While not a major weakness, it could be good to include an approximate idea of costs involved.
    *   **Limited discussion on transferability of the attack:** While there are experiments across different models and watermark combinations, a more thorough investigation into the transferability of the attack (e.g. can an attacker trained on one watermark be used to attack a different watermark?) could be useful.
    *   **Possible overstatement of worst-case effectiveness:** There's a risk that, like many attack papers, the specific attack presented here might be mitigated in future watermarking schemes. Therefore, the "fundamental threat" claim should be carefully interpreted, as it's based on the limitations of *current* watermarking approaches.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field of LLM security and watermarking. The adaptive robustness radius is a valuable tool for evaluating watermark robustness, and RLCracker provides a strong benchmark for assessing the security of watermarking schemes. The insights gained from this paper can guide the development of more robust and effective watermarking defenses.

**Score: 8.5**

**Justification:**

The paper provides a novel metric for assessing watermark robustness, a practical and highly effective attack method, and extensive experimental validation. The adaptive robustness radius is a sound theoretical contribution.  The RLCracker attack and the comprehensive experiments are strong practical contributions that clearly demonstrate the vulnerability of existing watermarking schemes. The highlighted role of system prompts and model reasoning further enhance the paper's value.  It is a substantial advance that will likely spur new directions in watermarking design. Some minor weaknesses like limited transferability and possibly overstating the long-term impact keep it from receiving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### SimDiff: Simulator-constrained Diffusion Model for Physically Plausible Motion Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20927v1)
- **Authors**: Akihisa Watanabe, Jiawei Ren, Li Siyao, Yichen Peng, Erwin Wu, Edgar Simo-Serra
- **Abstract**: Generating physically plausible human motion is crucial for applications such as character animation and virtual reality. Existing approaches often incorporate a simulator-based motion projection layer to the diffusion process to enforce physical plausibility. However, such methods are computationally expensive due to the sequential nature of the simulator, which prevents parallelization. We show that simulator-based motion projection can be interpreted as a form of guidance, either classifier-based or classifier-free, within the diffusion process. Building on this insight, we propose SimDiff, a Simulator-constrained Diffusion Model that integrates environment parameters (e.g., gravity, wind) directly into the denoising process. By conditioning on these parameters, SimDiff generates physically plausible motions efficiently, without repeated simulator calls at inference, and also provides fine-grained control over different physical coefficients. Moreover, SimDiff successfully generalizes to unseen combinations of environmental parameters, demonstrating compositional generalization.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and critical evaluation of the SimDiff paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SimDiff, a simulator-constrained diffusion model for generating physically plausible human motions.  Instead of relying on computationally expensive physics-based projections at each diffusion step (as in previous methods like PhysDiff), SimDiff learns to directly incorporate physical constraints (gravity, wind) into the denoising process. This is achieved by training lightweight adapters within a frozen diffusion model, conditioning on environment parameters.  The authors argue this is more efficient, allows for finer-grained control over physical coefficients, and demonstrates compositional generalization to unseen combinations of environmental parameters.  The key idea is to interpret simulator-based motion projection as a form of classifier guidance within the diffusion framework. The paper validates SimDiff on the HumanML3D dataset, showing improvements in physical plausibility and motion realism compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of SimDiff - directly integrating physical constraints into the diffusion process via lightweight adapters conditioned on environment parameters - represents a solid incremental advance. It departs from the paradigm of iterative physics projection, offering a more efficient solution. The theoretical reinterpretation of methods like PhysDiff as classifier guidance is also a valuable contribution, providing a framework for understanding and improving similar approaches. The demonstration of compositional generalization to unseen environmental conditions adds further novelty. However, the overall concept of using classifiers or guides within diffusion models is not entirely new, diminishing the impact on the novelty metric.
* **Significance:**  The significance lies in several aspects:
    * **Efficiency:** The efficiency improvement by avoiding repeated simulator calls during inference is significant, making the method more practical for real-time applications.
    * **Control:**  The ability to explicitly control environmental parameters offers a level of controllability that is often lacking in purely data-driven motion generation models.
    * **Generalization:** Demonstrating generalization to unseen environments is crucial for the real-world applicability of motion generation models. The compositional generalization is a strong positive feature.
    * **Practicality:** SimDiff seems to strike a better balance between physical plausibility and motion realism/textual alignment compared to methods that aggressively enforce physical constraints.
* **Strengths:**
    * Clear problem definition and motivation.
    * Solid theoretical grounding in classifier guidance.
    * Well-designed experiments and comparisons to relevant baselines.
    * Demonstrated improvements in efficiency and generalization.
    * Effective visualisations and supplementary materials to support claims.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Reliance on Simulated Data: As with many physics-based approaches, the model relies on simulated data for training, which may not perfectly reflect real-world physics and motion.  This could limit the realism of the generated motions to be very similar to its training data.
    * Limited Environmental Variety: Despite claiming diverse environment generalization, the tested environments only vary in gravity and planar wind, which is a very limited set of environments.  Many real world environments would have varying terrains and contact surfaces.
    * Small-scale Ablations: The ablation study has very little context and only tests at a handful of points. More scales should be tested along with a larger diversity of ablations.
    * Lack of Evaluation of the learned features: The paper doesn't do much evaluation of what the adapter blocks are doing. Are they learning something meaningful about the parameter space?
    * Lack of diversity in motions: Results are shown for only a single frame of animation. It is unclear how these motions evolve over time.

* **Potential Influence:**  SimDiff has the potential to influence future research in physically plausible motion generation by:
    * Shifting the focus from iterative physics projection to direct integration of constraints.
    * Highlighting the importance of controllable environmental parameters.
    * Providing a framework for evaluating compositional generalization in motion generation.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

Given the aforementioned points, SimDiff presents a solid incremental advancement in the field of physically plausible motion generation. The key strength is the efficiency gain, making it more practical. It offers improved control via environment parameters, which is significant. The reinterpretation of motion projection as classifier guidance is valuable. While the reliance on simulation data and the limited ablation study slightly detract from the overall impact, the compositional generalisation is a strong positive aspect of the paper. However, the scope of the environments evaluated and a lack of diversity is a major drawback and limit the significance, considering that this is a key claim in the paper.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Conditionally Whitened Generative Models for Probabilistic Time Series Forecasting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20928v1)
- **Authors**: Yanfeng Yang, Siwei Chen, Pingping Hu, Zhaotong Shen, Yingjie Zhang, Zhuoran Sun, Shuai Li, Ziqi Chen, Kenji Fukumizu
- **Abstract**: Probabilistic forecasting of multivariate time series is challenging due to non-stationarity, inter-variable dependencies, and distribution shifts. While recent diffusion and flow matching models have shown promise, they often ignore informative priors such as conditional means and covariances. In this work, we propose Conditionally Whitened Generative Models (CW-Gen), a framework that incorporates prior information through conditional whitening. Theoretically, we establish sufficient conditions under which replacing the traditional terminal distribution of diffusion models, namely the standard multivariate normal, with a multivariate normal distribution parameterized by estimators of the conditional mean and covariance improves sample quality. Guided by this analysis, we design a novel Joint Mean-Covariance Estimator (JMCE) that simultaneously learns the conditional mean and sliding-window covariance. Building on JMCE, we introduce Conditionally Whitened Diffusion Models (CW-Diff) and extend them to Conditionally Whitened Flow Matching (CW-Flow). Experiments on five real-world datasets with six state-of-the-art generative models demonstrate that CW-Gen consistently enhances predictive performance, capturing non-stationary dynamics and inter-variable correlations more effectively than prior-free approaches. Empirical results further demonstrate that CW-Gen can effectively mitigate the effects of distribution shift.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Conditionally Whitened Generative Models (CW-Gen) for probabilistic time series forecasting. CW-Gen aims to improve forecasting accuracy by incorporating prior information (conditional means and covariances) into generative models like diffusion models and flow matching. The core idea is to perform "conditional whitening," which involves subtracting the conditional mean and scaling by the inverse square root of the conditional covariance before applying the generative model. The paper provides theoretical justification for this approach, establishing sufficient conditions under which replacing the standard terminal distribution with a distribution parameterized by estimated conditional mean and covariance improves sample quality. The authors design a novel Joint Mean-Covariance Estimator (JMCE) to learn the conditional mean and sliding-window covariance simultaneously. They demonstrate empirically that CW-Gen consistently enhances predictive performance across various real-world datasets and improves resilience to distribution shifts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the concept of "conditional whitening" and its integration into generative time series models. While incorporating prior information into generative models is not entirely new (CARD, NsDiff, TsFlow), CW-Gen offers a distinct theoretical framework and a practical implementation through JMCE that appears to address some of the limitations of previous approaches. JMCE itself appears to provide improvements in stability, potentially due to the eigenvalue constraints.
