### Analysis of instruction-based LLMs' capabilities to score and judge text-input problems in an academic setting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20982v1)
- **Authors**: Valeria Ramirez-Garcia, David de-Fitero-Dominguez, Antonio Garcia-Cabot, Eva Garcia-Lopez
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) can act as evaluators, a role studied by methods like LLM-as-a-Judge and fine-tuned judging LLMs. In the field of education, LLMs have been studied as assistant tools for students and teachers. Our research investigates LLM-driven automatic evaluation systems for academic Text-Input Problems using rubrics. We propose five evaluation systems that have been tested on a custom dataset of 110 answers about computer science from higher education students with three models: JudgeLM, Llama-3.1-8B and DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B. The evaluation systems include: The JudgeLM evaluation, which uses the model's single answer prompt to obtain a score; Reference Aided Evaluation, which uses a correct answer as a guide aside from the original context of the question; No Reference Evaluation, which ommits the reference answer; Additive Evaluation, which uses atomic criteria; and Adaptive Evaluation, which is an evaluation done with generated criteria fitted to each question. All evaluation methods have been compared with the results of a human evaluator. Results show that the best method to automatically evaluate and score Text-Input Problems using LLMs is Reference Aided Evaluation. With the lowest median absolute deviation (0.945) and the lowest root mean square deviation (1.214) when compared to human evaluation, Reference Aided Evaluation offers fair scoring as well as insightful and complete evaluations. Other methods such as Additive and Adaptive Evaluation fail to provide good results in concise answers, No Reference Evaluation lacks information needed to correctly assess questions and JudgeLM Evaluations have not provided good results due to the model's limitations. As a result, we conclude that Artificial Intelligence-driven automatic evaluation systems, aided with proper methodologies, show potential to work as complementary tools to other academic resources.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a rigorous critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the application of large language models (LLMs) for automatically scoring and judging text-input problems (TIPs) in academic settings, aiming to provide automated and customized feedback to students. The authors explore five different evaluation systems, including JudgeLM evaluation, Reference Aided Evaluation, No Reference Evaluation, Additive Evaluation, and Adaptive Evaluation. These systems are tested on a dataset of 110 computer science answers from university students, comparing the results against human evaluation. The study finds that Reference Aided Evaluation performs best, demonstrating fair scoring and insightful evaluations. The paper also identifies the limitations of other methods, such as those caused by short answers and context limitations, and finds that models with proper methodologies can serve as complementary tools in education.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and increasingly important topic: the use of LLMs in education for automated assessment. The study's exploration of different evaluation methodologies is a strong point, offering a comparative analysis that sheds light on the strengths and weaknesses of each approach. The inclusion of a custom dataset of real student answers adds credibility to the findings.

However, the paper has some limitations:

*   **Limited Scope:** The dataset, while custom-made, is relatively small (110 answers) and focused solely on computer science.  This restricts the generalizability of the findings to other disciplines or types of assessment.

*   **Model Limitations:** The study primarily uses the Llama-3-1 8B model and JudgeLM, limiting its ability to provide strong evidence about how different models will perform in the different conditions.

*   **Methodological Nuances:**  While the descriptions of the different evaluation systems are generally clear, the precise prompts and implementation details could be described in a more granular way (included in the appendix). This makes replication more challenging.

*   **Lack of error analysis:** While statistics such as MAD and RMSE are given, providing a more qualitative error analysis would strengthen the significance of their conclusions.

Despite these limitations, the study offers valuable insights. The finding that Reference Aided Evaluation performs well aligns with the broader literature on LLM-as-a-judge and highlights the importance of providing LLMs with adequate context and guidelines for evaluation. The identification of weaknesses in Additive and Adaptive Evaluation provides a useful cautionary note about the challenges of imposing rigid structures on LLM judgment.

Overall, the paper makes a worthwhile contribution to the field, but further research with larger and more diverse datasets, more detailed descriptions, and more qualitative error analysis is needed to solidify the findings and explore the full potential of LLMs for automated assessment.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field, demonstrating a comparative analysis of methods for LLM-based text evaluation. The findings are practical, the methods are well described, and the paper is well-organized. However, its limitations with respect to dataset scope, model diversity, and methodological details restrict its overall impact and novelty. More extensive testing across diverse domains and increased depth of error analysis would warrant a higher score. The study offers a valuable starting point for further research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SiNGER: A Clearer Voice Distills Vision Transformers Further
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20986v1)
- **Authors**: Geunhyeok Yu, Sunjae Jeong, Yoonyoung Choi, Jaeseung Kim, Hyoseok Hwang
- **Abstract**: Vision Transformers are widely adopted as the backbone of vision foundation models, but they are known to produce high-norm artifacts that degrade representation quality. When knowledge distillation transfers these features to students, high-norm artifacts dominate the objective, so students overfit to artifacts and underweight informative signals, diminishing the gains from larger models. Prior work attempted to remove artifacts but encountered an inherent trade-off between artifact suppression and preserving informative signals from teachers. To address this, we introduce Singular Nullspace-Guided Energy Reallocation (SiNGER), a novel distillation framework that suppresses artifacts while preserving informative signals. The key idea is principled teacher feature refinement: during refinement, we leverage the nullspace-guided perturbation to preserve information while suppressing artifacts. Then, the refined teacher's features are distilled to a student. We implement this perturbation efficiently with a LoRA-based adapter that requires minimal structural modification. Extensive experiments show that \oursname consistently improves student models, achieving state-of-the-art performance in multiple downstream tasks and producing clearer and more interpretable representations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SINGER: A Clearer Voice Distills Vision Transformers Further."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses a critical issue in Vision Transformers (ViTs) related to knowledge distillation (KD): the presence of high-norm artifacts in teacher models that degrade the representation quality of student models when transferred through distillation.  These artifacts dominate the KD objective, leading students to overfit to them instead of learning informative signals. To overcome this, the authors propose Singular Nullspace-Guided Energy Reallocation (SINGER), a novel distillation framework.  SINGER refines teacher features by suppressing artifacts while preserving informative signals through nullspace-guided perturbations. A lightweight LoRA-based adapter efficiently implements this perturbation with minimal structural changes.  The results demonstrate improved performance on downstream tasks and generate clearer, more interpretable representations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its principled approach to mitigating high-norm artifacts in ViT knowledge distillation. Unlike previous methods that mask features randomly, SINGER strategically leverages the nullspace to suppress artifacts while retaining critical information. The LoRA-based adapter implementation provides an efficient means for applying this perturbation. The specific combination of nullspace guidance, energy reallocation, and LoRA adaptation distinguishes this work. This is a more sophisticated approach than existing masking or regularization techniques.

**Significance:** The problem addressed by the paper, the degradation of KD performance due to high-norm artifacts in ViTs, is important because it limits the potential benefits of larger teacher models and generalizability. A method to effectively reduce these artifacts contributes to improved transfer learning, more efficient model deployment, and enhanced interpretability. The significant gains shown across multiple downstream tasks demonstrate the potential impact of SINGER. The improved interpretability of feature maps could facilitate further research into ViT representations.

**Strengths:**

*   **Principled Approach:** The method is well-motivated and grounded in linear algebra, providing a clearer explanation for why and how artifacts are suppressed.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The consistent improvement across a diverse set of benchmarks (classification, semantic segmentation, depth estimation, domain shift) demonstrates the robustness of the approach. The paper also goes beyond simple accuracy metrics and provides qualitative results demonstrating improved feature map clarity.
*   **Efficient Implementation:** The LoRA adapter provides an efficient method of implementing the perturbation and minimizes structural changes.
*   **Thorough Ablation Studies:** Detailed ablation studies on the initialization method and loss terms help validate the contribution of each component to the overall performance.
*   **Careful Error Analyses:** They probe the nullspace properties in the appendix, providing valuable insights into the underlying mechanisms.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The paper primarily focuses on knowledge distillation between ViTs. The degree to which SINGER generalizes to other Transformer architectures or different modalities needs further investigation.
*   **Incremental rather than Transformative Novelty:** Although significant, the novelty builds on existing knowledge distillation and LoRA techniques, and is a targeted enhancement.
*   **Reliance on Linearization:** The method linearizes the FFN layer, which introduces an approximation that might not be fully accurate.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The method still involves hyperparameters (e.g., for the adapter), and the sensitivity analysis could be more extensive.
*   **Scalability of Nullspace Calculation:** For larger ViTs, calculating the nullspace might become computationally expensive. The computational cost of the nullspace analysis for extremely large models is not explored.

**Potential Influence:**

SINGER has the potential to influence the design of future KD methods for ViTs. The concept of artifact-aware distillation can encourage researchers to consider the specific properties of transformer models, leading to more effective distillation strategies. The nullspace-guided perturbation technique and adapter usage could also be adopted in other areas of transfer learning and model compression.

**Justification for Score:**

While SINGER presents a valuable contribution to ViT knowledge distillation, some limitations must be noted. The reliance on linear algebra approximations may restrict accuracy, and the high level of tuning is necessary.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### AOT*: Efficient Synthesis Planning via LLM-Empowered AND-OR Tree Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20988v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaozhuang Song, Xuanhao Pan, Xinjian Zhao, Hangting Ye, Shufei Zhang, Jian Tang, Tianshu Yu
- **Abstract**: Retrosynthesis planning enables the discovery of viable synthetic routes for target molecules, playing a crucial role in domains like drug discovery and materials design. Multi-step retrosynthetic planning remains computationally challenging due to exponential search spaces and inference costs. While Large Language Models (LLMs) demonstrate chemical reasoning capabilities, their application to synthesis planning faces constraints on efficiency and cost. To address these challenges, we introduce AOT*, a framework that transforms retrosynthetic planning by integrating LLM-generated chemical synthesis pathways with systematic AND-OR tree search. To this end, AOT* atomically maps the generated complete synthesis routes onto AND-OR tree components, with a mathematically sound design of reward assignment strategy and retrieval-based context engineering, thus enabling LLMs to efficiently navigate in the chemical space. Experimental evaluation on multiple synthesis benchmarks demonstrates that AOT* achieves SOTA performance with significantly improved search efficiency. AOT* exhibits competitive solve rates using 3-5$\times$ fewer iterations than existing LLM-based approaches, with the efficiency advantage becoming more pronounced on complex molecular targets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AOT* (AND-OR Tree Search with Generative Expansion), a novel framework for efficient retrosynthesis planning.  It combines the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) with systematic AND-OR tree search. AOT* maps LLM-generated complete synthesis routes onto AND-OR tree components, enabling efficient exploration via intermediate reuse and structural memory. It uses a retrieval-based context engineering approach to guide LLM generation. Experiments on several synthesis benchmarks demonstrate that AOT* achieves state-of-the-art performance with significantly improved search efficiency, often requiring 3-5x fewer iterations compared to existing LLM-based methods, especially on complex molecular targets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the *integration* of LLMs and AND-OR tree search in a specific and well-engineered way for retrosynthesis. While both LLMs and AND-OR tree search have been used separately, AOT*'s atomic mapping of complete LLM-generated synthesis routes *directly* into tree structures is a distinct contribution. The use of retrieval-augmented generation to provide in-context examples for the LLM is a common technique, but its careful integration within the AOT* framework adds to the method's overall effectiveness. The reward assignment strategy and context engineering are also presented as novel components that contribute to the method's efficiency. It doesn't present a groundbreaking architectural innovation in either LLMs or search, it focuses on practical application of existing techniques with a crucial integration.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant bottleneck in retrosynthesis planning: the computational cost associated with repeated neural network inference and the inefficiencies of tree search in large chemical spaces. By leveraging the reasoning ability of LLMs and then efficiently navigating the space with AND-OR trees, AOT* shows considerable improvement. The reported 3-5x efficiency gains are significant in practice, especially for high-throughput screening applications.  The ability to handle more complex molecular targets is also a strong point.  However, the reliance on LLMs still ties the performance to the quality of LLM generations. The well designed experiments and ablations convincingly show improvements compared to existing techniques.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition and Approach:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges in retrosynthesis planning and presents a well-defined solution.
    * **Systematic Integration:** The integration of LLMs and AND-OR tree search is systematic and carefully engineered.  The pathway-to-tree mapping and handling of structural constraints are explained in detail.
    * **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of AOT* on multiple benchmarks, surpassing existing methods.  The comparisons are made to other state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods and the use of several LLM architectures is useful. The thorough ablations add considerable value.
    * **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides detailed analysis of various aspects of the framework, including difficulty-stratified performance, efficiency analysis, and ablation studies.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on LLM quality:** Performance fundamentally depends on LLM generation quality, and a theoretical regret bound doesn't guarantee optimal synthesis. This weakness is inherent to LLM integration.
    * **Limited Theoretical Guarantees:**  UCB only guarantees near-optimal solutions. The gains come from structured sampling instead of guaranteeing an optimal outcome.
    * **Incremental Contribution:** AOT* combines well known techniques in a new and useful fashion, but it does not represent a fundamental theoretical breakthrough.
    * **Cost still can be a barrier:** While AOT* reduces iterations, overall cost for LLM API can be substantial with big computations.

* **Potential Influence:** AOT* has the potential to influence the field by providing a more practical and efficient approach to retrosynthesis planning. It could enable the discovery of novel synthetic routes for drug discovery and materials design, particularly for complex molecules. Furthermore, the framework's modular design allows it to be readily extended with other methods.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

AOT* is a solid engineering contribution that combines existing techniques in a novel and effective way to address a crucial problem in retrosynthesis. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating clear improvements in efficiency and performance. The paper has clear and complete problem definition, a sound technical approach, and well-designed experiments, adding up to a solid system design. However, it doesn't involve any substantial theoretical breakthroughs nor does it overcome the basic limitations inherent from relying on LLM performance and API costs. It’s a strong practical advance rather than a foundational change in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Binary Autoencoder for Mechanistic Interpretability of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20997v1)
- **Authors**: Hakaze Cho, Haolin Yang, Brian M. Kurkoski, Naoya Inoue
- **Abstract**: Existing works are dedicated to untangling atomized numerical components (features) from the hidden states of Large Language Models (LLMs) for interpreting their mechanism. However, they typically rely on autoencoders constrained by some implicit training-time regularization on single training instances (i.e., $L_1$ normalization, top-k function, etc.), without an explicit guarantee of global sparsity among instances, causing a large amount of dense (simultaneously inactive) features, harming the feature sparsity and atomization. In this paper, we propose a novel autoencoder variant that enforces minimal entropy on minibatches of hidden activations, thereby promoting feature independence and sparsity across instances. For efficient entropy calculation, we discretize the hidden activations to 1-bit via a step function and apply gradient estimation to enable backpropagation, so that we term it as Binary Autoencoder (BAE) and empirically demonstrate two major applications: (1) Feature set entropy calculation. Entropy can be reliably estimated on binary hidden activations, which we empirically evaluate and leverage to characterize the inference dynamics of LLMs and In-context Learning. (2) Feature untangling. Similar to typical methods, BAE can extract atomized features from LLM's hidden states. To robustly evaluate such feature extraction capability, we refine traditional feature-interpretation methods to avoid unreliable handling of numerical tokens, and show that BAE avoids dense features while producing the largest number of interpretable ones among baselines, which confirms the effectiveness of BAE serving as a feature extractor.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can provide a summary, a critical evaluation, and a score for the paper based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a novel autoencoder variant called the Binary Autoencoder (BAE) designed for mechanistic interpretability of large language models (LLMs).  The key idea is to enforce minimal entropy on minibatches of hidden activations, promoting feature independence and sparsity across instances. BAE uses a 1-bit discretization of hidden activations and gradient estimation for backpropagation to enable efficient entropy calculation.  The paper demonstrates two major applications of BAE: (1) Efficient estimation of entropy from hidden state sets for analyzing LLM inference dynamics and In-context Learning (ICL), and (2) Atomized feature untangling, showing BAE avoids dense features and extracts more interpretable features compared to baseline autoencoders like Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs). The paper also revises the traditional feature interpretation methods to be more robust against numerical token handling issues.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of binarized activations with an entropy-based training objective applied to minibatches, targeting global sparsity rather than instance-wise sparsity.  While binary neural networks and sparse autoencoders exist, the specific combination and application to mechanistic interpretability is a new contribution. The method of efficient entropy calculation on binarized activations to promote sparsity is also a novel approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial problem in mechanistic interpretability: the presence of dense, uninterpretable features in autoencoder-based feature extraction.  The significance stems from the potential to:

    *   Improve the interpretability of LLM internal representations, leading to a better understanding of their mechanisms.
    *   Provide a more efficient way to extract features, potentially enabling analysis of larger models.
    *   Offer a new perspective on LLM behaviors like ICL through the lens of information reduction.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly identifies a limitation of existing SAE-based approaches (lack of global sparsity).
    *   The proposed BAE is well-motivated, and the design choices (binarization, entropy objective) are justified.
    *   The paper presents empirical evidence supporting the effectiveness of BAE in both entropy estimation and feature untangling.
    *   The revision of feature interpretation methods to handle numerical tokens more robustly is a valuable contribution.
    *   The paper includes a detailed experimental setup, comparisons to relevant baselines, and ablation studies.
    *   The application to analyzing ICL as information reduction is novel and interesting.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The binarization may lead to information loss, potentially limiting the ability to extract more nuanced or complex features. (addressed partly by expanding dimensionality using a factor of 4x)
    *   The paper's evaluation relies heavily on a human-in-the-loop method (ComSem), which introduces subjectivity and may not scale well to larger models or feature sets. Although ComSem is more robust and improves upon direct reliance on LLMs for generating numerical outputs.
    *   The limitations section acknowledges some open questions, such as the non-zero reconstruction loss, natural language feature interpretation, and investigation scope (focus on smaller LLMs).
    *   The method might be sensitive to hyperparameter tuning (entropy loss weight, batch size, etc.)
    *   Lack of theoretical analysis for convergence/optimality.
*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   BAE could become a useful tool for researchers in mechanistic interpretability, providing a more effective way to extract and analyze features in LLMs.
    *   The information-theoretic perspective on LLM behavior, especially ICL, could inspire new research directions.
    *   The revised feature interpretation methods could improve the reliability of automatic feature analysis pipelines.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper introduces a novel and well-motivated approach to address a key challenge in mechanistic interpretability. The empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness of BAE, and the paper offers valuable insights into LLM behavior. The weaknesses are acknowledged and provide avenues for future research. While binarization introduces potential information loss, the paper makes good arguments about benefits. Considering novelty, significance, and overall quality, I would rate this paper as a strong contribution to the field, but with some limitations that prevent it from achieving a truly exceptional score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Single Neuron Works: Precise Concept Erasure in Text-to-Image Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21008v1)
- **Authors**: Qinqin He, Jiaqi Weng, Jialing Tao, Hui Xue
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image models exhibit remarkable capabilities in image generation. However, they also pose safety risks of generating harmful content. A key challenge of existing concept erasure methods is the precise removal of target concepts while minimizing degradation of image quality. In this paper, we propose Single Neuron-based Concept Erasure (SNCE), a novel approach that can precisely prevent harmful content generation by manipulating only a single neuron. Specifically, we train a Sparse Autoencoder (SAE) to map text embeddings into a sparse, disentangled latent space, where individual neurons align tightly with atomic semantic concepts. To accurately locate neurons responsible for harmful concepts, we design a novel neuron identification method based on the modulated frequency scoring of activation patterns. By suppressing activations of the harmful concept-specific neuron, SNCE achieves surgical precision in concept erasure with minimal disruption to image quality. Experiments on various benchmarks demonstrate that SNCE achieves state-of-the-art results in target concept erasure, while preserving the model's generation capabilities for non-target concepts. Additionally, our method exhibits strong robustness against adversarial attacks, significantly outperforming existing methods.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Single Neuron-based Concept Erasure (SNCE), a novel method for precisely removing harmful concepts from text-to-image diffusion models. The method leverages sparse autoencoders (SAEs) to identify individual neurons in the text encoder responsible for generating unwanted content, such as nudity or violence. By selectively suppressing the activation of these specific neurons, SNCE aims to achieve targeted concept erasure with minimal impact on overall image quality and the ability to generate non-target concepts.  The paper presents experiments demonstrating the effectiveness of SNCE in removing nudity and violence, while maintaining good image quality and robustness against adversarial attacks. It also includes a comprehensive analysis of the manipulation coefficient and neuron selection settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The central idea of targeting individual neurons for concept erasure is relatively novel. Prior concept erasure methods often operate at a much coarser granularity, modifying larger portions of the model. Using sparse autoencoders to identify concept-specific neurons and then selectively intervening on those neurons offers a finer degree of control that appears promising. This targeted manipulation provides a more precise control over the erasure process.
* **Significance:** The ability to remove harmful concepts from text-to-image models is of great importance for ensuring safety and ethical use.  If SNCE can achieve this with high precision and minimal collateral damage, it represents a significant step forward. The results presented seem to support the effectiveness of the approach, particularly with the adversarial testing. This capability could significantly impact content moderation and responsible AI development.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Precise Concept Erasure:** The primary strength of SNCE is its ability to achieve precise concept erasure by targeting specific neurons. The results show an improvement over existing methods in removing nudity while preserving image quality.
    * **Robustness to Adversarial Attacks:** The method demonstrates strong robustness against adversarial attacks, which is crucial for practical deployment. The superior performance compared to baseline methods in the presence of prompts crafted specifically to bypass safety mechanisms is a significant advantage.
    * **Scalable Manipulation:** The scalable manipulation mechanism, which allows fine-grained control on concept intensity through manipulation coefficients and neuron selection, is a valuable feature.
    * **Interpretability:** Utilizing SAEs to identify neurons potentially adds to the interpretability of the method. By understanding which neurons contribute to harmful concepts, researchers can gain insights into the model's inner workings.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **SAE Training Dependency:** The method relies on training a sparse autoencoder. The performance of SNCE is therefore linked to the effectiveness of the SAE. While the paper describes the SAE training process, the sensitivity of SNCE to variations in SAE architecture and training data should be investigated. This could be an overhead in application.
    * **Generalization to Other Concepts:** While the paper demonstrates results on nudity and violence, it would be beneficial to evaluate the method's performance on a broader range of harmful concepts. Further experiments will be needed to confirm its generalizability.
    * **Computational Cost:** While not explicitly stated, training SAEs is typically computationally intensive. Quantifying the computational overhead of SNCE compared to other concept erasure methods would be important.
    * **Potential for Misuse:** While intended for safety, the same technique could potentially be used for malicious purposes (e.g., selectively removing certain features to promote biases).

* **Justification of Score:**

While the method exhibits strengths in targeted concept removal and robustness, several potential limitations require further investigation. The paper's novelty lies in the neuron-level manipulation aspect, but the reliance on SAEs and the need for comprehensive experimentation across different concepts and model architectures temper its immediate impact. It's also crucial to address the potential for misuse. The method is therefore considered a worthwhile advancement in the field but not yet a paradigm shift.

### RollPacker: Mitigating Long-Tail Rollouts for Fast, Synchronous RL Post-Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21009v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Gao, Yuheng Zhao, Dakai An, Tianyuan Wu, Lunxi Cao, Shaopan Xiong, Ju Huang, Weixun Wang, Siran Yang, Wenbo Su, Jiamang Wang, Lin Qu, Bo Zheng, Wei Wang
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement Learning (RL) is a pivotal post-training technique for enhancing the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). However, synchronous RL post-training often suffers from significant GPU underutilization, referred to as bubbles, caused by imbalanced response lengths within rollout steps. Many RL systems attempt to alleviate this problem by relaxing synchronization, but this can compromise training accuracy. In this paper, we introduce tail batching, a novel rollout scheduling strategy for synchronous RL that systematically consolidates prompts leading to long-tail responses into a small subset of rollout steps (long rounds), while ensuring that the majority of steps (short rounds) involve only balanced, short rollouts. By excluding long responses from short rounds and rescheduling them into a few designated long rounds, tail batching effectively reduces GPU idle time during rollouts and significantly accelerates RL training without sacrificing accuracy. We present RollPacker, a system that fully harnesses the benefits of tail batching through holistic optimizations across all three RL stages: elastic parallelism adaptation for rollout, dynamic resource allocation and scheduling for reward, and stream-based training. Empirical results show that RollPacker achieves a 2.03x-2.56x end-to-end training time reduction compared to veRL and up to 2.24x speedup compared to RLHFuse for the Qwen2.5 family of LLMs on up to 128 H800 GPUs.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize and critically evaluate the paper "RollPacker: Mitigating Long-Tail Rollouts for Fast, Synchronous RL Post-Training."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses a significant bottleneck in synchronous Reinforcement Learning (RL) post-training for Large Language Models (LLMs): GPU underutilization due to imbalanced response lengths (long-tail rollouts). The authors propose "tail batching," a novel rollout scheduling strategy that consolidates prompts leading to long-tail responses into dedicated "long rounds" while ensuring that the majority of steps ("short rounds") comprise balanced, short rollouts. The paper introduces RollPacker, a system implementing tail batching with optimizations across all three RL stages: elastic parallelism adaptation for rollout, dynamic resource allocation and scheduling for reward, and stream-based training.  Experiments on Qwen2.5 LLMs show RollPacker achieves significant end-to-end training time reductions compared to existing systems like veRL and RLHFuse.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of tail batching is novel and directly addresses a well-known problem in synchronous RL post-training for LLMs.  While techniques exist to overlap stages or relax synchronization, the systematic approach of consolidating long-tail responses into separate rounds is a clever way to maintain on-policy training semantics while improving GPU utilization. Speculative execution within short rounds and deferral to long rounds for tail prompts adds a practical implementation aspect.  The system-level optimizations are also important, demonstrating the need for a holistic approach.

**Significance:** The problem of GPU underutilization in synchronous RL post-training is highly significant, given the increasing size of LLMs and the computational cost of RL fine-tuning.  RollPacker's ability to significantly reduce training time has direct practical implications, allowing for faster development and iteration of LLMs.  The experimental results, demonstrating substantial speedups compared to established baselines on real-world datasets and models, strengthens the paper's claims and suggests its potential impact on the field.  The paper provides a thorough breakdown of how various optimizations in RollPacker contribute to the end-to-end performance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of GPU underutilization in synchronous RL post-training.
*   **Novel Solution:** Tail batching is a novel and well-motivated approach to address the problem.
*   **Holistic System Design:** RollPacker provides a complete system, addressing bottlenecks in all three RL stages.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents compelling experimental results, demonstrating significant speedups compared to existing systems.
*   **Thorough Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough analysis of the performance benefits of each optimization.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Implementation Complexity:** The system design, particularly the parallelism planner and stream trainer, seems relatively complex, potentially increasing the barrier to adoption. While the paper attempts to provide detailed descriptions, the algorithms could benefit from additional clarification in the Appendix.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** Speculative execution has to make assumptions about the response length of tail prompts. A poor selection of parameters will impact short rounds and long round performance. The paper could offer more guidance on selecting and tuning these hyperparameters.
*   **Limited Generalization:** While the experiments are performed on the Qwen2.5 family of LLMs, further investigation is needed to assess the generalizability of RollPacker to other LLM architectures and datasets. It would be beneficial to test this on publicly available open source LLMs.
*   **Focus on Synchronous RL:** The paper explicitly targets synchronous RL. While this is a common approach, future work could explore how tail batching could be adapted or integrated into asynchronous RL frameworks, which offer other potential benefits.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses of the paper, I assign it a score of **8**. The core idea of tail batching and the holistic system design are significant contributions to the field of RL post-training for LLMs. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating the practical benefits of the approach. However, the implementation complexity, hyperparameter sensitivity, and limited generalizability warrant a slightly lower score. Overall, this paper offers valuable insights and practical solutions to a critical problem, positioning it as a notable advancement in the field.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 8/10

### Automatic Red Teaming LLM-based Agents with Model Context Protocol Tools
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21011v1)
- **Authors**: Ping He, Changjiang Li, Binbin Zhao, Tianyu Du, Shouling Ji
- **Abstract**: The remarkable capability of large language models (LLMs) has led to the wide application of LLM-based agents in various domains. To standardize interactions between LLM-based agents and their environments, model context protocol (MCP) tools have become the de facto standard and are now widely integrated into these agents. However, the incorporation of MCP tools introduces the risk of tool poisoning attacks, which can manipulate the behavior of LLM-based agents. Although previous studies have identified such vulnerabilities, their red teaming approaches have largely remained at the proof-of-concept stage, leaving the automatic and systematic red teaming of LLM-based agents under the MCP tool poisoning paradigm an open question. To bridge this gap, we propose AutoMalTool, an automated red teaming framework for LLM-based agents by generating malicious MCP tools. Our extensive evaluation shows that AutoMalTool effectively generates malicious MCP tools capable of manipulating the behavior of mainstream LLM-based agents while evading current detection mechanisms, thereby revealing new security risks in these agents.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Automatic Red Teaming LLM-based Agents with Model Context Protocol Tools":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the emerging security risks associated with the integration of Model Context Protocol (MCP) tools in LLM-based agents. It focuses on tool poisoning attacks, where malicious instructions are injected into the metadata of MCP tools, potentially manipulating the behavior of LLM-based agents. The authors argue that existing red teaming approaches are primarily proof-of-concept and lack automation. To address this gap, they propose AUTOMALTOOL, an automated red teaming framework that generates malicious MCP tools to evaluate the vulnerability of LLM-based agents. The framework consists of four agents: Initial Generator, Oracle, Effectiveness Evaluator, and Tool Optimizer, which collaborate to create evasive and effective malicious MCP tools. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating AUTOMALTOOL's ability to generate malicious MCP tools, evade existing detection methods, and induce intended malicious behaviors in real-world LLM-based agents like Claude Desktop and Cline.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach by automating the red teaming process for LLM-based agents specifically targeting MCP tool poisoning. While prior work has identified the vulnerability and explored simple examples, AUTOMALTOOL offers a systematic and automated framework, which is a significant step forward. The multi-agent system design, with its collaborative approach for generating, evaluating, and optimizing malicious tools, is a key innovative aspect. The paper takes the existing knowledge around tool poisoning and elevates it to a more robust and practical application.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its ability to uncover and demonstrate new security risks within LLM-based agents utilizing MCP tools. The experiments highlight that widely used LLM-based agents are vulnerable to these attacks, and existing detection mechanisms are largely ineffective. The findings are important for AI agent developers and security practitioners, raising awareness about the potential threats and the need for more robust defenses. By providing an automated red teaming framework, the paper empowers developers to proactively identify and mitigate vulnerabilities in their systems. This is significant as it shifts the focus from reactive to proactive security measures in the rapidly evolving field of LLM-based agent security.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Automation:** The framework automates the generation of malicious MCP tools, overcoming the limitations of manual approaches.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes thorough experiments with real-world LLM-based agents and MCP servers.
    *   **Evasion Techniques:** The generated malicious tools exhibit strong evasion capabilities against current detection methods.
    *   **Multi-Agent Design:** The collaborative multi-agent system ensures both effectiveness and evasiveness of malicious tools.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Malicious Behaviors:** The study focuses on two types of malicious behaviors (incorrect parameter invocation and output results misinterpretation). Other potential attacks are not explored.
    *   **Black-Box Assumption:** The assumption of a black-box scenario may limit the applicability of the framework in situations where the attacker has access to some internal details of the agent.
    *   **Simulated Environment:** While the experiments use real LLM-based agents, the simulated environment for effectiveness evaluation may not fully capture the complexities of real-world interactions.
    *   **Limited LLMs and LLM Backend Model Evaluations**: While the choice of the LLMs used in the study is appropriate in that these are well known and widely used models, the evaluations could have used a wider range of LLMs for the red teaming exercises. In addition, the number of LLM backend models is also quite limited.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Driving the development of more robust detection and defense mechanisms for MCP tool poisoning.
    *   Encouraging AI agent developers to incorporate automated red teaming in their security practices.
    *   Informing the design of more secure LLM-based agents that are resilient to tool poisoning attacks.
    *   Stimulating further research on the security risks associated with LLM-based agents and MCP tools.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of LLM security by automating the red teaming process for MCP tool poisoning. While it has some limitations in terms of scope and evaluation environment, the paper presents a novel framework with strong experimental results that demonstrates the feasibility and impact of the approach. The paper uncovers significant vulnerabilities in widely used LLM-based agents and highlights the need for more effective detection methods. The multi-agent system design and the focus on generating evasive malicious tools are particularly noteworthy. AUTOMALTOOL framework's contribution for understanding the potential security risks associated with MCP within LLM-based agent technology is significant.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Predicting LLM Reasoning Performance with Small Proxy Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21013v1)
- **Authors**: Woosung Koh, Juyoung Suk, Sungjun Han, Se-Young Yun, Jay Shin
- **Abstract**: Given the prohibitive cost of pre-training large language models, it is essential to leverage smaller proxy models to optimize datasets before scaling up. However, this approach becomes challenging for reasoning capabilities, which exhibit emergent behavior that only appear reliably at larger model sizes, often exceeding 7B parameters. To address this, we introduce rBridge, showing that small proxies ($\leq$1B) can effectively predict large-model reasoning by aligning more closely with (1) the pre-training objective and (2) the target task. rBridge achieves this by weighting negative log-likelihood with task alignment, using reasoning traces from frontier models as gold labels. In our experiments, rBridge (i) reduces dataset ranking costs by over 100x relative to the best baseline, (ii) achieves the strongest correlation across six reasoning benchmarks at 1B to 32B scale, and (iii) zero-shot transfers predictive relationships across pre-training datasets at 1B to 7B scale. These findings indicate that rBridge offers a practical path for exploring reasoning-oriented pre-training at lower cost.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RBRIDGE, a method designed to predict the reasoning performance of large language models (LLMs) using smaller proxy models (≤1B parameters). The core idea behind RBRIDGE is to align the proxy model more closely with both the pre-training objective and the target reasoning task. This is achieved by weighting the negative log-likelihood (NLL) loss with task alignment, using reasoning traces from larger, "frontier" models as gold labels. The authors demonstrate that RBRIDGE can significantly reduce dataset ranking costs, improve correlation between proxy and large model performance across various reasoning benchmarks (up to 32B parameters), and even enable zero-shot transfer of predictive relationships across pre-training datasets.  The key innovation lies in using the reasoning trace of a larger model as the gold standard for the proxy's learning objective, and further weighting the tokens based on their perceived importance to the task.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its specific approach to aligning small proxy models with large models in the context of *reasoning*. While using proxy models and scaling laws is not entirely new, the particular technique of leveraging reasoning traces from larger models as gold labels for NLL and weighting tokens based on their contribution to the final answer appears to be novel. The work addresses a critical challenge: the difficulty of predicting emergent reasoning abilities, which often only reliably appear in larger models.

**Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact how LLMs are pre-trained and optimized. The high cost of pre-training large models makes efficient dataset selection and pre-training strategies crucial. RBRIDGE provides a practical way to explore reasoning-oriented pre-training at a lower cost, potentially accelerating the development of more capable LLMs. The gains in dataset ranking cost reduction and the improved correlation between proxy and large model performance are compelling. Also the ability to perform zero-shot functional relationship transfer adds value and economic benefits.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of predicting reasoning performance with small proxy models and provides a strong motivation based on the prohibitive cost of large-scale pre-training.
*   **Novel Approach:** The proposed RBRIDGE method is well-defined and leverages insightful observations about the importance of alignment with both the pre-training objective and the target task. The use of frontier model reasoning traces as gold labels is a clever idea.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments across multiple benchmarks and model sizes. The results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of RBRIDGE in dataset ranking, performance prediction, and zero-shot transfer.
*   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies help to understand the contribution of each component of the RBRIDGE method.
*   **Practical Implications:** The paper highlights the potential for RBRIDGE to be used in a two-stage dataset optimization framework, further increasing its practical significance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on Frontier Models:** RBRIDGE relies on the availability of reasoning traces from larger, "frontier" models. This introduces a dependency on the quality and availability of these models, which might not always be the case.  It is worth noting that such dependance could limit the approach's wider applicability.
*   **Reasoning Trace Extraction Limitations:** The format in which the frontier models extract reasoning traces can lead to issues, which could create limitations, even though a method is proposed to address this.
*   **Limited Exploration of Model Architectures:** The paper primarily focuses on scaling data and doesn't explicitly explore the impact of different model architectures at the proxy level.
*   **Limited Real-world datasets and benchmark:** The data exploration might be limited as only publicly available datasets are used, also the number of benchmarks used are limited.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   Encouraging the development of more efficient and cost-effective pre-training strategies for LLMs.
*   Providing a practical framework for exploring reasoning-oriented pre-training.
*   Inspiring further research on alignment methods for proxy models.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8** to this paper. The work demonstrates considerable novelty and significance by addressing the core challenge of how to efficiently train large models. The proposed RBRIDGE approach has the potential to significantly reduce the computational cost involved in the pre-training of LLMs, especially in terms of dataset selection and pre-training strategy optimization. The empirical evaluation is comprehensive and convincing. However, there are some limitations. Dependence on frontier models does limit applicability.  The reliance on publicly available datasets may also make the study less reflective of real-world data. Despite these limitations, the work makes a valuable contribution to the field.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Actor-Critic without Actor
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21022v1)
- **Authors**: Donghyeon Ki, Hee-Jun Ahn, Kyungyoon Kim, Byung-Jun Lee
- **Abstract**: Actor-critic methods constitute a central paradigm in reinforcement learning (RL), coupling policy evaluation with policy improvement. While effective across many domains, these methods rely on separate actor and critic networks, which makes training vulnerable to architectural decisions and hyperparameter tuning. Such complexity limits their scalability in settings that require large function approximators. Recently, diffusion models have recently been proposed as expressive policies that capture multi-modal behaviors and improve exploration, but they introduce additional design choices and computational burdens, hindering efficient deployment. We introduce Actor-Critic without Actor (ACA), a lightweight framework that eliminates the explicit actor network and instead generates actions directly from the gradient field of a noise-level critic. This design removes the algorithmic and computational overhead of actor training while keeping policy improvement tightly aligned with the critic's latest value estimates. Moreover, ACA retains the ability to capture diverse, multi-modal behaviors without relying on diffusion-based actors, combining simplicity with expressiveness. Through extensive experiments on standard online RL benchmarks,ACA achieves more favorable learning curves and competitive performance compared to both standard actor-critic and state-of-the-art diffusion-based methods, providing a simple yet powerful solution for online RL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Actor-Critic Without Actor" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Actor-Critic without Actor (ACA), a novel reinforcement learning framework that eliminates the explicit actor network commonly found in actor-critic methods. Instead, ACA generates actions directly from the gradient field of a noise-level critic, which estimates action values at different noise levels within a denoising process. This approach is inspired by guidance techniques used in diffusion-based models. The key benefits of ACA are simplified architecture, reduced computational overhead, and tight alignment between policy improvement and the critic's value estimates. ACA is demonstrated to achieve competitive or superior performance compared to standard actor-critic and diffusion-based RL methods on MuJoCo benchmarks, while requiring fewer parameters and being more sample efficient.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper's core idea – eliminating the actor network in actor-critic methods and leveraging a noise-level critic for direct action generation – offers a promising step towards simplifying and improving RL algorithms. The authors have successfully demonstrated, through comprehensive experiments, the effectiveness of ACA compared to strong baselines, indicating that it does achieve its stated goals of improved sample efficiency and reduced complexity. Here's a deeper dive:

*   **Novelty:** The approach appears relatively novel, especially the specific way in which the noise-level critic is used to guide the denoising process to generate actions *directly*, bypassing the need for a separate actor network. Although guidance and diffusion have been explored, ACA’s integration of these concepts in online RL with the removal of the actor seems non-trivial. While noise-level critics are known, specifically linking the gradient to guide denoising is a fresh approach.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the potential to make RL more accessible and efficient, particularly in complex environments where large function approximators are needed. The reduced parameter count and improved sample efficiency are critical for real-world deployment, where resources are often constrained. However, the complexity traded for computational steps (iterative denoising) and stability by the gradient normalization is a potential bottleneck and has to be considered.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Simplicity:** The framework is conceptually simple and removes a potentially unstable element (the actor) in traditional actor-critic methods.
    *   **Efficiency:** ACA is more parameter-efficient, leading to faster training and reduced memory requirements.
    *   **Performance:** The empirical results demonstrate competitive or superior performance on standard benchmarks.
    *   **Multimodal behavior:** Effectively captures multi-modal behaviors through critic guided denoising.
    *   **Code and reproducibility:** The release of code will greatly aid adoption and facilitate further research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** The iterative denoising process might introduce a computational bottleneck, especially in environments with very high-dimensional action spaces, which needs to be empirically considered and theoretically analyzed to understand where it breaks.
    *   **Guidance weight tuning:** The reliance on manually tuning the guidance weight 'w' and noise-level requires expertise and may limit the method's broader applicability.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While Proposition 1 provides some theoretical justification, the paper lacks deeper theoretical guarantees regarding convergence and stability, which would strengthen its claims.
    *   **Parameter sensitivity:** The claim in Section 4.3 regarding manual selection of the weighting factor *w* can make the process difficult and can lead to less broad adoption. More detailed analysis is needed.
*   **Impact:** ACA has the potential to influence the design of future RL algorithms by encouraging simpler architectures and tighter integration between policy evaluation and improvement. Diffusion models have shown promise for exploration, however, deployment of such models in an actor network can cause issues, and leveraging guidance is a potential approach to counter that issue.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** The paper presents a genuinely interesting idea with solid empirical validation. The simplified architecture and promising performance gains merit attention. However, the limitations around guidance parameter tuning and theoretical guarantees prevent it from being considered a truly breakthrough contribution. The trade-off between removing the actor and the computational overhead of the denoising process need to be further explored for it to be a truly effective solution. If future work addresses the manual tuning aspect, provides deeper theoretical analysis, and addresses computational bottlenecks in the process of noise-level-based guidance, ACA could have a considerable impact on the field. Therefore, a score of 7 reflects the paper's significant potential and solid empirical evidence, while acknowledging the current limitations that need to be addressed in future research.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### KeyWorld: Key Frame Reasoning Enables Effective and Efficient World Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21027v1)
- **Authors**: Sibo Li, Qianyue Hao, Yu Shang, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Robotic world models are a promising paradigm for forecasting future environment states, yet their inference speed and the physical plausibility of generated trajectories remain critical bottlenecks, limiting their real-world applications. This stems from the redundancy of the prevailing frame-to-frame generation approach, where the model conducts costly computation on similar frames, as well as neglecting the semantic importance of key transitions. To address this inefficiency, we propose KeyWorld, a framework that improves text-conditioned robotic world models by concentrating transformers computation on a few semantic key frames while employing a lightweight convolutional model to fill the intermediate frames. Specifically, KeyWorld first identifies significant transitions by iteratively simplifying the robot's motion trajectories, obtaining the ground truth key frames. Then, a DiT model is trained to reason and generate these physically meaningful key frames from textual task descriptions. Finally, a lightweight interpolator efficiently reconstructs the full video by inpainting all intermediate frames. Evaluations on the LIBERO benchmark demonstrate that KeyWorld achieves a 5.68$\times$ acceleration compared to the frame-to-frame generation baseline, and focusing on the motion-aware key frames further contributes to the physical validity of the generated videos, especially on complex tasks. Our approach highlights a practical path toward deploying world models in real-time robotic control and other domains requiring both efficient and effective world models. Code is released at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/Keyworld-E43D.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "KeyWorld," a novel framework designed to enhance the efficiency and effectiveness of text-conditioned robotic world models.  The core idea is to shift computational focus from generating every frame of a video to generating only a sparse set of keyframes representing significant transitions in the robot's motion.  A lightweight convolutional neural network then interpolates the intermediate frames.  KeyWorld leverages the Ramer-Douglas-Peucker (RDP) algorithm to extract these motion-aware keyframes from robot pose trajectories.  A Diffusion Transformer (DiT) is trained to generate the keyframes based on textual task descriptions and initial observations. The lightweight CNN interpolates the missing frames.  Experiments on the LIBERO benchmark demonstrate a significant speedup compared to a frame-to-frame generation baseline while maintaining, and sometimes improving, the physical plausibility of the generated videos.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficiency:** The primary strength is the significant reduction in computational cost. The demonstrated speedups are compelling and make world models more viable for real-time applications.
    *   **Motion-Aware Keyframes:** The RDP-based keyframe selection is a smart way to capture critical physical transitions, potentially leading to more physically grounded and semantically meaningful representations.
    *   **Modular Design:** The decoupling of keyframe generation and interpolation into separate modules is a good design choice, allowing for potential improvements to each component independently.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The results on the LIBERO benchmark are well-presented and support the claims of efficiency and improved physical plausibility. The ablation studies further validate the design choices.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** The reliance on CogVideoX as the backbone of the keyframe generator makes the method somewhat dependent on the advancements in general video generation. While fine-tuning is performed, the inherited biases or limitations of CogVideoX could still be present.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** While the overall framework is novel, the individual components (DiT, CNN-based interpolation) are not entirely new. The primary contribution lies in their integration and adaptation to the robotic world model setting.
    *   **Potential for Artifacts in Interpolation:**  The effectiveness of the interpolation relies on the motion between keyframes being relatively simple. While the RDP algorithm aims for this, there's a risk of introducing artifacts or inaccuracies if the robot's movements are highly complex within a given keyframe interval, or if the target differs between frames.
    *   **Manual Object-Level Accuracy:** Object-level accuracy assessment using manual inspection may be prone to bias. Automatic methods can ensure better reliability.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a crucial bottleneck in robotic world models: computational cost. By making these models more efficient, it opens the door for more practical applications in real-time control and online planning.
    *   The focus on physical plausibility is important for ensuring the reliability of the world model's predictions, particularly in downstream tasks like policy learning.
    *   The framework could inspire other researchers to explore similar keyframe-based approaches in different domains.
*   **Novelty:** The overall framework, specifically the combination of motion-aware keyframe selection, diffusion-based keyframe generation, and lightweight interpolation, is the key element of novelty. While individual components are not necessarily groundbreaking, the integrated approach offers a practical and effective solution to a challenging problem.  Prior works have explored keyframe selection based on task decomposition or uniform sampling.  KeyWorld's focus on *physical motion* as the basis for keyframe extraction is what gives it an edge.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-designed framework that addresses a critical challenge in robotic world models. The improvements in efficiency and physical plausibility are significant, and the experimental results support the claims. While the novelty of individual components is limited, their integration and adaptation to the robotic domain are well-executed. The weaknesses related to potential artifacts from interpolation and dependence on existing video generation architectures should not be ignored. The manual object accuracy metrics are susceptible to bias. The overall impact of the technique may depend on future developments in the base model.
Score: 7.5
The assigned score indicates a strong contribution to the field, showcasing a novel and effective approach to improve robotic world models. While not a fundamental breakthrough in individual components, the framework's significance is in its practical applicability and the clever combination of techniques that leads to a tangible improvement in efficiency and video quality.
- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Who Gets Cited Most? Benchmarking Long-Context Language Models on Scientific Articles
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21028v1)
- **Authors**: Miao Li, Alexander Gurung, Irina Saparina, Mirella Lapata
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces SciTrek, a novel question-answering benchmark designed to evaluate the long-context reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) using scientific articles. Current long-context benchmarks often rely on non-scientific texts, focus on simple information retrieval tasks, or employ artificial contexts. SciTrek addresses these limitations by proposing complex questions that require information aggregation and synthesis across multiple full-text scientific articles. Questions and their ground-truth answers are automatically generated by formulating them as SQL queries over a database constructed from article metadata (titles, authors, and references). The SQL operations provide explicit, verifiable reasoning steps for fine-grained error analysis, and the construction process scales to contexts up to 1M tokens with minimal supervision. Extensive experiments on a diverse set of open-weight and proprietary LLMs demonstrate that SciTrek poses a significant challenge as the context length increases, with supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning offering only limited gains. Our analysis reveals systematic shortcomings in models' abilities to perform basic numerical operations and accurately locate specific information in long contexts.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces SciTrek, a new question-answering benchmark for evaluating the long-context reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) using scientific articles. SciTrek addresses limitations of existing benchmarks by using full-text scientific articles, posing complex questions requiring information aggregation and synthesis, and automatically generating questions and answers through SQL queries over article metadata. The SQL operations provide explicit reasoning steps, and the construction process scales to 1M tokens with minimal supervision. Experiments on various LLMs demonstrate that SciTrek poses a significant challenge, even with fine-tuning and reinforcement learning. The analysis reveals shortcomings in models' numerical abilities and information localization in long contexts.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and important problem: the need for better benchmarks to evaluate the long-context reasoning capabilities of LLMs, particularly in scientific domains. While many existing benchmarks focus on non-scientific text or simple information retrieval, SciTrek attempts to fill this gap.

**Novelty:**

The key novelty lies in the methodology for generating questions and answers. Formulating question generation as SQL queries over article metadata is a clever approach that offers several advantages:

*   **Automatic Generation:** Allows for the creation of large datasets with minimal human effort, which is crucial for long-context evaluation.
*   **Scalability:** Can be easily scaled to larger datasets and longer contexts.
*   **Explicit Reasoning Steps:** SQL queries provide verifiable reasoning steps, facilitating error analysis.

However, the novelty is somewhat tempered by the fact that the questions posed in the benchmark are relatively superficial and focus on metadata rather than deep content understanding. While this allows for the rigorous evaluation of information retrieval and aggregation skills, it doesn't fully capture the complex reasoning involved in scientific literature review. There are some works like the one from (Li et al, 2023) which also address QA systems with SQL. The novelty in this paper comes from generating question answering datasets from scientific articles.

**Significance:**

The potential significance of SciTrek is in its ability to drive research on long-context reasoning in scientific domains. By providing a challenging and scalable benchmark with explicit reasoning steps, it can help researchers identify the strengths and weaknesses of LLMs and develop better models for scientific tasks. The analysis of model failures, particularly the struggles with numerical operations and logical constructs, provides valuable insights for future research directions.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Methodology:** The SQL-based question generation is a significant strength.
*   **Scalability:** The benchmark can be easily scaled to larger datasets and longer contexts.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough analysis of model failures.
*   **Focus on Scientific Domain:** Addresses a gap in existing benchmarks.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Superficial Questions:** The questions primarily focus on metadata and do not require deep content understanding.
*   **Limited Complexity of SQL Queries:** While SQL provides explicit reasoning steps, the queries are relatively simple and do not capture the full complexity of scientific reasoning.
*   **Dataset biases:** Since LLMs are used to generate the questions, there is a risk the LLMs may show some biases when answering those questions.

**Potential Influence:**

SciTrek has the potential to influence the field by:

*   Providing a challenging benchmark for long-context reasoning in scientific domains.
*   Driving research on improving the numerical abilities and logical reasoning of LLMs.
*   Inspiring new methodologies for automatic question generation.

However, its influence may be limited by the relative simplicity of the questions and the focus on metadata.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses discussed above, a score of 7 is appropriate. The methodology for question generation is novel and scalable, and the benchmark addresses a relevant problem. However, the limitations of the questions (superficiality and lack of deep content understanding) temper the overall significance. The paper provides a valuable contribution to the field, but further work is needed to develop benchmarks that capture the full complexity of scientific reasoning.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### FORCE: Transferable Visual Jailbreaking Attacks via Feature Over-Reliance CorrEction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21029v1)
- **Authors**: Runqi Lin, Alasdair Paren, Suqin Yuan, Muyang Li, Philip Torr, Adel Bibi, Tongliang Liu
- **Abstract**: The integration of new modalities enhances the capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) but also introduces additional vulnerabilities. In particular, simple visual jailbreaking attacks can manipulate open-source MLLMs more readily than sophisticated textual attacks. However, these underdeveloped attacks exhibit extremely limited cross-model transferability, failing to reliably identify vulnerabilities in closed-source MLLMs. In this work, we analyse the loss landscape of these jailbreaking attacks and find that the generated attacks tend to reside in high-sharpness regions, whose effectiveness is highly sensitive to even minor parameter changes during transfer. To further explain the high-sharpness localisations, we analyse their feature representations in both the intermediate layers and the spectral domain, revealing an improper reliance on narrow layer representations and semantically poor frequency components. Building on this, we propose a Feature Over-Reliance CorrEction (FORCE) method, which guides the attack to explore broader feasible regions across layer features and rescales the influence of frequency features according to their semantic content. By eliminating non-generalizable reliance on both layer and spectral features, our method discovers flattened feasible regions for visual jailbreaking attacks, thereby improving cross-model transferability. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our approach effectively facilitates visual red-teaming evaluations against closed-source MLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FORCE: TRANSFERABLE VISUAL JAILBREAKING ATTACKS VIA FEATURE OVER-RELIANCE CORRECTION":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of limited transferability in optimization-based visual jailbreaking attacks on Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). It identifies that these attacks tend to rely on model-specific features, residing in high-sharpness regions of the loss landscape and overemphasizing semantically poor frequency components, making them sensitive to parameter changes and thus hindering transfer to other MLLMs. To mitigate this, the authors propose a method called Feature Over-Reliance Correction (FORCE). FORCE guides the attack towards broader feasible regions in earlier layer features using a layer-aware regularisation, and rescales the influence of frequency features to reduce the over-reliance on high-frequency components.  Experiments demonstrate that FORCE improves the cross-model transferability of visual jailbreaking attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a novel perspective on why visual jailbreaking attacks lack transferability.  Identifying the over-reliance on model-specific features in early layers and the undue influence of high-frequency components as key factors is a meaningful contribution. The FORCE method, with its layer-aware regularization and spectral rescaling, is also a novel approach for improving transferability.
*   **Significance:**  Improving the transferability of visual jailbreaking attacks is significant for red-teaming MLLMs.  Because closed-source commercial MLLMs are generally black boxes, having methods to evaluate their safety through transferable attacks is valuable. The paper provides insights into the vulnerabilities of MLLMs and offers a practical method for more robust security assessments. The identification of high-sharpness loss landscapes and feature over-reliance offers directions for future research into making MLLMs more robust.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper's analysis of the loss landscape and feature representations of visual jailbreaking attacks is thorough and well-supported by empirical evidence.
    *   The proposed FORCE method is well-motivated and addresses the identified limitations of existing attacks.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate consistent and substantial improvements in transferability across diverse MLLM architectures and datasets, strengthening the practical value of the method.
    *   The ablation studies convincingly demonstrate the contribution of each component of FORCE.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    * While the results showcase enhanced transferability, the absolute attack success rates on some commercial models remain relatively low, suggesting room for further improvement. This suggests that while FORCE addresses the over-reliance issue, there may be other factors limiting attack success on highly robust models.
    * The method involves several hyperparameters (N, η, λ, M) which requires additional tuning. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of hyperparameter selection and sensitivity analysis.
    * There's a lack of in-depth qualitative analysis to understand what type of features the improved attacks are exploiting after applying FORCE.

*   **Potential Impact:** This paper could significantly influence the field of MLLM security. It provides a valuable methodology for red-teaming these models and reveals potential vulnerabilities that need to be addressed. The paper’s insights on feature reliance can inform the design of more robust MLLMs. While the goal is jailbreaking, the insights might also have implications for more general transfer learning and adversarial robustness in multimodal models.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of MLLM security by providing a novel analysis of the transferability challenges in visual jailbreaking attacks and proposing a practical method to address these limitations.  The strengths of the paper outweigh its weaknesses, and the results demonstrate a significant improvement in the transferability of visual jailbreaking attacks.
