### It's Not You, It's Clipping: A Soft Trust-Region via Probability Smoothing for LLM RL
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21282v1)
- **Authors**: Madeleine Dwyer, Adam Sobey, Adriane Chapman
- **Abstract**: Training large language models (LLMs) with reinforcement learning (RL) methods such as PPO and GRPO commonly relies on ratio clipping to stabilise updates. While effective at preventing instability, clipping discards information and introduces gradient discontinuities. We propose Probability Smoothing Policy Optimisation (PSPO), which smooths the current policy's probabilities toward the old (behaviour) policy before computing the importance ratio, analogous to label smoothing. Unlike clipping, PSPO preserves gradient signal, while interpolation toward the old policy creates a soft trust region that discourages large, destabilising updates, with formal guarantees. We instantiate PSPO within GRPO (GR-PSPO) and fine-tune Qwen2.5-0.5B and Qwen2.5-1.5B on GSM8K, evaluating on GSM8K test and the cross-dataset generalisation on SVAMP, ASDiv, and MATH-500. Relative to unclipped GRPO (single iteration; no data reuse, ratio always = 1), GR-PSPO achieves similar performance but improves the reasoning leading to clearer and more concise responses which are more logical. Compared to clipped GRPO, GR-PSPO substantially improves performance both the 0.5B and 1.5B models, with a boost of over 20% on GSM8K (39.7% vs. 17.6% for 0.5B, 59.4% vs. 37.8% for 1.5B).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Probability Smoothing Policy Optimization (PSPO), a novel approach for stabilizing reinforcement learning (RL) when fine-tuning large language models (LLMs).  PSPO replaces the common ratio clipping technique in methods like PPO and GRPO with a smoothing mechanism that interpolates the current policy's probabilities with those of the old (behavior) policy. This smoothing acts as a soft trust region, discouraging large, potentially destabilizing updates, while preserving gradient information that is lost in clipping. The authors instantiate PSPO within GRPO (GR-PSPO) and evaluate it on mathematical reasoning tasks using the Qwen2.5 LLM. They demonstrate significant performance improvements over clipped GRPO and comparable performance to unclipped GRPO with improved response quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of replacing ratio clipping with probability smoothing, inspired by label smoothing in supervised learning, is reasonably novel in the context of LLM fine-tuning with RL. It directly addresses the limitations of clipping (vanishing gradients, potential for missing better policies) by providing a continuous gradient signal. The formulation of probability smoothing as a soft trust region with implicit KL-divergence control adds theoretical depth.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in offering a practical and more stable alternative to ratio clipping for LLM fine-tuning. LLM training is known for its instability, and PSPO aims to alleviate this issue. The performance improvements, especially the significant gains over clipped GRPO and improved response quality compared to unclipped GRPO, underscore the potential practical value of this approach. Furthermore, PSPO is claimed to be computationally efficient.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of instability associated with ratio clipping in LLM RL fine-tuning.
    *   **Principled Approach:** The proposed solution, PSPO, is well-motivated and grounded in established concepts such as label smoothing and trust regions.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results on multiple mathematical reasoning datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of GR-PSPO compared to established baselines. The focus on T=0 is a good decision, since that reflects greedy decoding.
    *   **Analysis of Response Quality:** The inclusion of LLM-as-Judge metrics to evaluate response quality is important, as it goes beyond simple accuracy and assesses the coherence and clarity of the generated responses.
    *   **Detailed comparisons**: the paper clearly provides and compares the results with existing literature.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is primarily focused on mathematical reasoning tasks, which, although relevant, may not fully capture the nuances of other LLM applications. The paper mentions that mathematical reasoning has a binary, objective reward signal and suggests future research can explore domains with continuous rewards.
    *   **Model Size:** The experiments are conducted using models up to 1.5B parameters. While representative, it would be valuable to see how PSPO scales to larger models, where instability issues are often more pronounced. The paper also fails to address the potential increased compute/memory required for using a policy model.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While PSPO offers advantages over clipped GRPO, its quantitative improvements compared to unclipped GRPO (albeit with better qualitative results) might be considered incremental in some scenarios.
    *   **Lack of Ablation studies:** The paper would benefit from more detailed ablation studies. For instance, investigating the impact of different smoothing strengths or comparing PSPO to other regularization techniques would strengthen the analysis.
    *  **Overclaiming a Soft Trust Region:** While the KL upper bound is technically proven in the lemma, the paper could explore in more detail whether the contraction on the ratio *actually* results in similar behaviour to KL penalties.

*   **Potential Influence:** PSPO has the potential to influence the field of LLM fine-tuning by providing a more stable and effective alternative to ratio clipping. The simplicity and computational efficiency of PSPO make it attractive for adoption in practical applications. It might also inspire further research into alternative regularization techniques for LLM RL.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-motivated approach (PSPO) to improve the stability of LLM fine-tuning with RL. It offers a practical and computationally efficient alternative to ratio clipping and demonstrates promising empirical results on mathematical reasoning tasks. While the evaluation scope is limited to smaller models and a specific domain, the potential benefits of PSPO in stabilizing LLM training and improving response quality make it a valuable contribution to the field. However, more exploration for diverse reward settings is needed.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Bounds of Chain-of-Thought Robustness: Reasoning Steps, Embed Norms, and Beyond
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21284v1)
- **Authors**: Dingzirui Wang, Xuanliang Zhang, Keyan Xu, Qingfu Zhu, Wanxiang Che, Yang Deng
- **Abstract**: Existing research indicates that the output of Chain-of-Thought (CoT) is significantly affected by input perturbations. Although many methods aim to mitigate such impact by optimizing prompts, a theoretical explanation of how these perturbations influence CoT outputs remains an open area of research. This gap limits our in-depth understanding of how input perturbations propagate during the reasoning process and hinders further improvements in prompt optimization methods. Therefore, in this paper, we theoretically analyze the effect of input perturbations on the fluctuation of CoT outputs. We first derive an upper bound for input perturbations under the condition that the output fluctuation is within an acceptable range, based on which we prove that: (i) This upper bound is positively correlated with the number of reasoning steps in the CoT; (ii) Even an infinitely long reasoning process cannot eliminate the impact of input perturbations. We then apply these conclusions to the Linear Self-Attention (LSA) model, which can be viewed as a simplified version of the Transformer. For the LSA model, we prove that the upper bound for input perturbation is negatively correlated with the norms of the input embedding and hidden state vectors. To validate this theoretical analysis, we conduct experiments on three mainstream datasets and four mainstream models. The experimental results align with our theoretical analysis, empirically demonstrating the correctness of our findings.
- **Summary**: The paper presents a theoretical analysis of the robustness of Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning in large language models (LLMs) to input perturbations. It derives an upper bound for input perturbations that keep output fluctuation within an acceptable range, showing that: (i) the upper bound correlates positively with the number of reasoning steps, and (ii) even infinite reasoning steps cannot eliminate the effect of input perturbations. The paper then applies these findings to the Linear Self-Attention (LSA) model, proving that the upper bound for input perturbations is negatively correlated with the norms of input embeddings and hidden state vectors. Experimental validation across several datasets and models supports the theoretical analysis. Finally, they utilize the results to improve prompt selection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper offers a valuable theoretical perspective on CoT robustness, an area previously dominated by empirical observations. Deriving an upper bound for input perturbations is a significant contribution, providing a formal framework for understanding CoT's sensitivity. The insights concerning the impact of reasoning steps, input/hidden state norms, and model-level factors are novel and can guide the development of more robust CoT techniques.

However, the paper has some limitations. The reliance on the Lipschitz continuity assumption, while common, may not fully capture the complexity of LLMs. Also, the LSA model, while useful for theoretical analysis, is a simplified version of the Transformer, potentially limiting the generalizability of some conclusions. The practical implication and efficacy of their improved prompt selection method could be more extensively explored and compared to a wider range of optimization methods.

**Novelty:** The theoretical analysis is relatively novel, providing a new angle to a previously empirical area. The connection to input and hidden state norms is interesting and could be a valuable area of future work.

**Significance:** The paper has the potential to influence the design and optimization of CoT prompting strategies. A more theoretical understanding of robustness can guide better prompt engineering and possibly contribute to building more resilient LLMs.

**Justification of Score:** While the paper provides a useful contribution, it's still primarily theoretical. Further work needs to explore the practical ramifications and broader implications. The assumptions used are typical for the area but might not fully capture the behavior of current LLMs, therefore limiting the value and generalizability of the conclusions.

### VC-Agent: An Interactive Agent for Customized Video Dataset Collection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21291v1)
- **Authors**: Yidan Zhang, Mutian Xu, Yiming Hao, Kun Zhou, Jiahao Chang, Xiaoqiang Liu, Pengfei Wan, Hongbo Fu, Xiaoguang Han
- **Abstract**: Facing scaling laws, video data from the internet becomes increasingly important. However, collecting extensive videos that meet specific needs is extremely labor-intensive and time-consuming. In this work, we study the way to expedite this collection process and propose VC-Agent, the first interactive agent that is able to understand users' queries and feedback, and accordingly retrieve/scale up relevant video clips with minimal user input. Specifically, considering the user interface, our agent defines various user-friendly ways for the user to specify requirements based on textual descriptions and confirmations. As for agent functions, we leverage existing multi-modal large language models to connect the user's requirements with the video content. More importantly, we propose two novel filtering policies that can be updated when user interaction is continually performed. Finally, we provide a new benchmark for personalized video dataset collection, and carefully conduct the user study to verify our agent's usage in various real scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of our agent for customized video dataset collection. Project page: https://allenyidan.github.io/vcagent_page/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VC-Agent: An Interactive Agent for Customized Video Dataset Collection":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VC-Agent, an interactive agent based on Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) designed to streamline the process of collecting customized video datasets from the internet. The agent interacts with users to refine their requirements through an iterative process of video proposals, confirmations, and comments.  VC-Agent uses two key policies: a Template-Based Acceptance Policy (learning from accepted videos) and an Attribute-Aware Rejection Policy (learning from rejected videos and user comments). This iterative refinement process allows the agent to adapt its filtering criteria, ultimately automating the collection of a tailored video dataset. The authors provide a new benchmark for personalized video dataset collection and user studies to demonstrate the agent's effectiveness and efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using an interactive agent with MLLMs for customized dataset collection is relatively novel. While MLLMs have been applied to video understanding and generation, the specific application to interactive dataset curation, with feedback loops and policy updating, is a valuable contribution. The design of Template-Based Acceptance Policy and Attribute-Aware Rejection Policy, and integration with MLLM for reasoning, is also novel, though the individual components are not new.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem: the time-consuming and labor-intensive nature of creating specialized video datasets. By automating this process, the agent can significantly accelerate research and development in various video-related tasks. The proposed benchmark, while limited to three domains, provides a valuable resource for evaluating future systems for personalized video dataset creation. The demonstrated performance gains over existing methods, including traditional video retrieval and generic MLLMs, support the significance of the work.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The iterative interaction mechanism with policy updating is a well-designed approach for capturing nuanced user requirements.
    *   The design of two updatable policies is a good approach.
    *   The paper presents a new benchmark, supporting the field.
    *   The user studies demonstrate the practical benefits of VC-Agent, showing reduced time and increased satisfaction compared to manual collection.
    *   Ablation studies justify the design choices for the agent.
    *   The project page provides code, web UI, benchmarks and the dataset produced by the agent.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The benchmark, while valuable, is limited to three domains, limiting its broader applicability. A more comprehensive benchmark with diverse and challenging requirements would strengthen the paper further.
    *   The specific reliance on LLAVA and a specific grounding model somewhat limits the generality of the approach. An analysis of how the system would perform with different LLMs and grounding models would be beneficial.
    *   Limitations in handling certain complex video properties and user intentions are also mentioned in the paper which also affect the rating of the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of future systems for data curation, particularly in the video domain. The interactive agent approach could be extended to other data types and tasks. The proposed benchmark provides a basis for comparing and evaluating new approaches.

**Justification:**

VC-Agent introduces a compelling framework for customized video dataset collection, leveraging the strengths of MLLMs in a novel interactive setting. The iterative refinement process and policy updates significantly enhance the efficiency of constructing custom datasets. While the benchmark has limitations and reliance on existing MLLMs reduces the innovativeness of the methodology, the problem that it solves is highly relevant and well structured in the paper. The results presented are good enough, and there is a complete demonstration provided along with the code, web UI, etc.

### Semantic Clustering of Civic Proposals: A Case Study on Brazil's National Participation Platform
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21292v1)
- **Authors**: Ronivaldo Ferreira, Guilherme da Silva, Carla Rocha, Gustavo Pinto
- **Abstract**: Promoting participation on digital platforms such as Brasil Participativo has emerged as a top priority for governments worldwide. However, due to the sheer volume of contributions, much of this engagement goes underutilized, as organizing it presents significant challenges: (1) manual classification is unfeasible at scale; (2) expert involvement is required; and (3) alignment with official taxonomies is necessary. In this paper, we introduce an approach that combines BERTopic with seed words and automatic validation by large language models. Initial results indicate that the generated topics are coherent and institutionally aligned, with minimal human effort. This methodology enables governments to transform large volumes of citizen input into actionable data for public policy.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of efficiently organizing and analyzing citizen contributions on Brazil's national participation platform, Brasil Participativo.  The authors propose a methodology that combines BERTopic, a neural topic modeling technique, with seed words derived from the Vocabulário Controlado de Governo Eletrônico (VCGE), the official Brazilian government's controlled vocabulary.  Furthermore, they leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) for automatic topic validation and interpretation.  The results indicate that this hybrid approach generates coherent, diverse, and institutionally aligned topics, enabling governments to process large volumes of citizen input into actionable data for public policy formulation. The paper explores both unsupervised and semi-supervised approaches, ultimately demonstrating the superior performance of the semi-supervised method using VCGE seed words.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Practical Problem & Relevant Context:** The paper tackles a highly relevant and practical problem. Governments worldwide struggle to process and utilize citizen input from digital platforms. The specific context of Brasil Participativo and the VCGE adds a layer of practical significance, addressing a real-world need.
    * **Methodological Soundness:** The methodology appears well-designed. The use of BERTopic, seed words, and LLMs is a logical combination, and the evaluation metrics (Coherence, Diversity, ARI, NMI) are appropriate for assessing topic quality and alignment with the VCGE. The careful parameter tuning and model selection process (BERTimbau-large identified as the best embedding model) increases the credibility of the results.  The comparison of unsupervised and semi-supervised approaches provides a strong justification for the chosen methodology.
    * **Clear Presentation & Evaluation:** The paper is well-written and presents the methodology and results clearly. The use of tables and figures aids in understanding the performance comparisons. The questions being addressed were clear from the start, and the results were clearly related back to the original research questions.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Limited Novelty in Technique Combination:** While the application is novel, the individual techniques (BERTopic, seed words, LLMs) are established. The combination of these techniques, while effective, might not be considered a groundbreaking methodological innovation.  The originality in the technical aspects might be not very high.
    * **Dependency on VCGE:** The reliance on the VCGE as a ground truth is both a strength and a weakness. It ensures institutional alignment but also limits the potential for discovering truly novel insights that might not be captured by the existing vocabulary. This could hinder the identification of emerging trends or citizen concerns that are not already reflected in the official taxonomy. The method's generalizability is hindered somewhat by its dependence on a pre-existing controlled vocabulary.
    * **Modest Gains in Some Metrics:**  While the semi-supervised approach improves Coherence and ARI, the gains in some metrics (e.g., NMI) are relatively small. The relatively small loss in diversity with semi-supervision should be highlighted, but one may hope that the improvement is higher.

* **Significance and Impact:**

    * **Potential for Real-World Application:** The paper has strong potential for real-world impact. The methodology could be directly implemented in Brazil and adapted for similar citizen participation platforms in other countries. The efficiency gains achieved by automating topic extraction and validation could significantly improve the responsiveness of governments to citizen concerns.
    * **Contribution to the Field:** The work contributes to the field of Natural Language Processing (NLP) by demonstrating a practical application of topic modeling and LLMs in the public policy domain. It shows how these techniques can be used to bridge the gap between citizen input and government decision-making.  The focus on aligning with institutional taxonomies is a valuable contribution, as it addresses a common challenge in applying NLP to government data.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by demonstrating a practical and effective method for processing citizen input on digital platforms. While the individual techniques are not entirely novel, the combination of BERTopic, seed words, and LLMs, along with the focus on institutional alignment, addresses a real-world challenge in the public policy domain. The clear presentation, sound methodology, and potential for real-world application enhance the significance of the work. Considering both the strengths and weaknesses, the paper represents a solid contribution with practical implications. However, the somewhat limited novelty and reliance on the VCGE prevent it from achieving a higher score.

### Quantized Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21302v1)
- **Authors**: Weilun Feng, Haotong Qin, Mingqiang Wu, Chuanguang Yang, Yuqi Li, Xiangqi Li, Zhulin An, Libo Huang, Yulun Zhang, Michele Magno, Yongjun Xu
- **Abstract**: Learning-based 3D reconstruction models, represented by Visual Geometry Grounded Transformers (VGGTs), have made remarkable progress with the use of large-scale transformers. Their prohibitive computational and memory costs severely hinder real-world deployment. Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) has become a common practice for compressing and accelerating models. However, we empirically observe that PTQ faces unique obstacles when compressing billion-scale VGGTs: the data-independent special tokens induce heavy-tailed activation distributions, while the multi-view nature of 3D data makes calibration sample selection highly unstable. This paper proposes the first Quantization framework for VGGTs, namely QuantVGGT. This mainly relies on two technical contributions: First, we introduce Dual-Smoothed Fine-Grained Quantization, which integrates pre-global Hadamard rotation and post-local channel smoothing to mitigate heavy-tailed distributions and inter-channel variance robustly. Second, we design Noise-Filtered Diverse Sampling, which filters outliers via deep-layer statistics and constructs frame-aware diverse calibration clusters to ensure stable quantization ranges. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate that QuantVGGT achieves the state-of-the-art results across different benchmarks and bit-width, surpassing the previous state-of-the-art generic quantization method with a great margin. We highlight that our 4-bit QuantVGGT can deliver a 3.7$\times$ memory reduction and 2.5$\times$ acceleration in real-hardware inference, while maintaining reconstruction accuracy above 98\% of its full-precision counterpart. This demonstrates the vast advantages and practicality of QuantVGGT in resource-constrained scenarios. Our code is released in https://github.com/wlfeng0509/QuantVGGT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QuantVGGT, a novel quantization framework designed specifically for Visual Geometry Grounded Transformers (VGGTs). VGGTs, while powerful for 3D reconstruction, are computationally expensive. QuantVGGT addresses this by enabling low-bit quantization (W4A4) without significantly compromising reconstruction accuracy.  The framework introduces two key components:  Dual-Smoothed Fine-Grained Quantization (DSFQ) to handle skewed activation distributions caused by data-independent tokens, and Noise-Filtered Diverse Sampling (NFDS) to ensure stable quantization ranges by filtering outliers and creating frame-aware calibration clusters.  Experimental results demonstrate that QuantVGGT achieves state-of-the-art performance in camera pose and point map estimation, surpassing generic quantization methods with substantial memory reduction and inference acceleration.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its targeted approach to quantizing VGGTs.  It identifies and addresses the specific challenges associated with VGGTs: the impact of data-independent tokens and the instability of calibration due to the multi-view 3D data nature.  The proposed DSFQ and NFDS are technically sound and specifically designed to overcome these challenges.  While rotation-based quantization and noise filtering techniques exist, their application within the 3D reconstruction domain, tailored to the VGGT architecture, is a significant contribution.  The dual-stage smoothing combining global rotation and local channel smoothing is a nuanced and potentially generalizable technique. The adaptive and diverse noise filtering coupled with frame-aware clustering for quantization range stability further advances the state of the art.
*   **Significance:** QuantVGGT has the potential to significantly impact the deployment of VGGTs in resource-constrained scenarios. The demonstrated 3.7x memory reduction and 2.5x acceleration at minimal accuracy loss are highly valuable. The work tackles a crucial problem – the computational barrier of large 3D reconstruction models – making them more accessible for real-world applications. The performance gains are not merely incremental; the results show a substantial improvement over existing generic quantization methods, particularly at low bit-widths. The impact on camera pose and point map estimation demonstrated across various data conditions underscores the potential impact within practical scenarios.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Identification:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of quantizing VGGTs, highlighting issues that are often overlooked by generic quantization methods.
*   **Technical Soundness:** DSFQ and NFDS are well-motivated and technically implemented, with clear explanations and justifications for each component.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are thorough, covering multiple benchmarks, bit-widths, and ablation studies.  The comparison to strong baselines demonstrates the superiority of QuantVGGT.
*   **Practical Impact:**  The paper emphasizes the real-world benefits of QuantVGGT, such as reduced memory footprint and increased inference speed. The algorithm is shown to be applicable to consumer GPUs with strong results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Generalizability Beyond VGGT:** The paper's focus is highly specific to VGGTs. While DSFQ and NFDS may have broader applicability, their effectiveness in other 3D reconstruction architectures or even general vision transformers is not explicitly demonstrated.  Further analysis showcasing their application in diverse computer vision scenarios could have strengthened the contribution.
*   **Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper includes the theoretical grounding for noise filtering with the data clustering approach, there is limited information regarding the specific conditions where improvements can be expected.
*   **Hardware Validation:** While GPU tests are provided, actual deployment on edge devices could further showcase practical advantages.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper's potential influence is significant. It provides a blueprint for quantizing large 3D reconstruction models and introduces techniques that could be adapted to other architectures.  It will likely stimulate further research in efficient 3D reconstruction and inspire the development of more specialized quantization techniques for other domains.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a strong contribution with significant novelty and potential impact. It tackles a specific problem in a technically sound and well-evaluated manner. While its generalizability is somewhat limited, the specific challenges addressed and the degree of improvement achieved justify a high score. The impact within the 3D reconstruction community will be tangible.

### Sycophancy Is Not One Thing: Causal Separation of Sycophantic Behaviors in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21305v1)
- **Authors**: Daniel Vennemeyer, Phan Anh Duong, Tiffany Zhan, Tianyu Jiang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) often exhibit sycophantic behaviors -- such as excessive agreement with or flattery of the user -- but it is unclear whether these behaviors arise from a single mechanism or multiple distinct processes. We decompose sycophancy into sycophantic agreement and sycophantic praise, contrasting both with genuine agreement. Using difference-in-means directions, activation additions, and subspace geometry across multiple models and datasets, we show that: (1) the three behaviors are encoded along distinct linear directions in latent space; (2) each behavior can be independently amplified or suppressed without affecting the others; and (3) their representational structure is consistent across model families and scales. These results suggest that sycophantic behaviors correspond to distinct, independently steerable representations.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "Sycophancy is Not One Thing: Causal Separation of Sycophantic Behaviors in LLMs" investigates whether sycophantic behaviors in large language models (LLMs) arise from a single underlying mechanism or multiple distinct processes. The authors decompose sycophancy into sycophantic agreement and sycophantic praise and contrast both with genuine agreement.  Using difference-in-means directions, activation additions, and subspace geometry, they show that the three behaviors are encoded along distinct linear directions in the latent space, each can be independently amplified or suppressed without affecting the others, and their representational structure is consistent across model families and scales. The findings suggest that sycophantic behaviors correspond to distinct, independently steerable representations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty of this paper lies in its explicit decomposition of sycophancy into distinct subtypes and the mechanistic investigation of their representations and causal relationships within LLMs. Previous work has acknowledged sycophancy but has largely treated it as a monolithic phenomenon or focused on individual subtypes without comparing them. The paper's exploration of the internal representations of sycophantic behaviors and the use of causal interventions to demonstrate their independence is a significant step forward. Prior works, while contributing to the understanding of steerability, have approached sycophancy in a more generalized manner, lacking the nuanced distinctions that this research provides. The work is also enhanced by its exploration of the geometrical properties between SyA, GA and SyPr across layers which leads to interesting internal separation of SYA and GA.

**Significance:**

The significance of this paper stems from its implications for understanding and mitigating harmful behaviors in LLMs. By demonstrating that sycophantic behaviors are not a single phenomenon, the paper suggests that targeted interventions can be developed to reduce harmful sycophancy without negatively affecting other desirable behaviors like honest agreement. The ability to selectively control different aspects of sycophancy has the potential to enhance the safety and reliability of LLMs. By showing that targeted and controlled mitigations are possible, it moves towards a better deployment safety story.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition and Scope:** The paper clearly defines the scope of its investigation and focuses on specific, measurable aspects of sycophancy.
*   **Rigorous Methodology:** The use of multiple methods (DiffMean, activation additions, subspace geometry) provides strong evidence for the conclusions.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are well-designed and conducted on multiple models and datasets, which supports the generalizability of the findings.
*   **Clear and Well-Supported Conclusions:** The authors are careful to qualify their conclusions and provide ample evidence to support them.
*   **Reproducibility:** The release of code and data promotes reproducibility and further research in this area.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Synthetic Datasets:**  The heavy reliance on synthetic datasets, while allowing for controlled experimentation, raises questions about the extent to which the findings generalize to more complex, real-world scenarios and datasets where ground-truth is ambiguous. Although the analysis on TruthfulQA offers some insight, more experimentation is required.
*   **Limited Scope of Behaviors:** While the paper focuses on two specific sycophantic behaviors (agreement and praise), sycophancy may manifest in other ways. Further research could explore additional subtypes of sycophancy.
*   **Intervention Method:** The activation addition is not a silver bullet and might lead to unforeseen effects.

**Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **8** to this paper.

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the understanding of sycophancy in LLMs by demonstrating the functional separability of different sycophantic behaviors. The research is methodologically sound, well-executed, and the findings are clearly presented. The implications of this work for targeted intervention strategies are potentially significant. The main weakness is the over-reliance on synthetic datasets. While the TruthfulQA dataset provides some evidence of external validity, it is not sufficient. More analysis must be performed to determine if these observations hold in real-world situations. If that is proven, this paper could be viewed as a groundbreaking paper in the field. The ability to intervene safely to sycophantic behaviours is of utmost importance for model deployment.

### SAGE: A Realistic Benchmark for Semantic Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21310v1)
- **Authors**: Samarth Goel, Reagan J. Lee, Kannan Ramchandran
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) achieve strong performance on traditional benchmarks, there is an urgent need for more challenging evaluation frameworks that probe deeper aspects of semantic understanding. We introduce SAGE (Semantic Alignment & Generalization Evaluation), a rigorous benchmark designed to assess both embedding models and similarity metrics across five categories: Human Preference Alignment, Transformation Robustness, Information Sensitivity, Clustering Performance, and Retrieval Robustness. Unlike existing benchmarks that focus on isolated capabilities, SAGE evaluates semantic understanding through adversarial conditions, noisy transformations, and nuanced human judgment tasks across 30+ datasets. Our comprehensive evaluation of 9 embedding models and classical metrics reveals significant performance gaps, with no single approach excelling across all dimensions. For instance, while state-of-the-art embedding models like OpenAI's text-embedding-3-large dominate in aligning with human preferences (0.682 vs. 0.591 for the best classical metric), they are significantly outperformed by classical metrics on information sensitivity tasks, where Jaccard Similarity achieves a score of 0.905 compared to the top embedding score of 0.794. SAGE further uncovers critical trade-offs: OpenAI's text-embedding-3-small achieves the highest clustering performance (0.483) but demonstrates extreme brittleness with the lowest robustness score (0.011). SAGE exposes critical limitations in current semantic understanding capabilities and provides a more realistic assessment of model robustness for real-world deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SAGE (Semantic Alignment & Generalization Evaluation), a new benchmark designed to evaluate semantic understanding in large language models (LLMs) and embedding models.  SAGE assesses models across five categories: Human Preference Alignment, Transformation Robustness, Information Sensitivity, Clustering Performance, and Retrieval Robustness.  Unlike existing benchmarks that focus on ideal conditions and narrow tasks, SAGE evaluates models under adversarial conditions, noisy transformations, and with nuanced human judgment tasks across a diverse set of datasets (30+). The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation of nine embedding models and several classical similarity metrics, revealing performance gaps and trade-offs that are not apparent in traditional benchmarks.  For example, while OpenAI's `text-embedding-3-large` aligns well with human preferences, it struggles with information sensitivity compared to simpler metrics like Jaccard Similarity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the holistic and adversarial nature of the benchmark.  While existing benchmarks like MTEB and BEIR are valuable, they often focus on retrieval or isolated aspects of semantic understanding. SAGE's integration of robustness, information sensitivity, and human alignment tasks provides a more realistic and challenging evaluation framework. The effort to create adversarial conditions and transformations also contributes to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it exposes limitations in current semantic understanding capabilities that are masked by existing benchmarks. It demonstrates that models performing well on standard benchmarks can exhibit brittleness in real-world noisy environments. This is a critical insight for practitioners deploying LLMs and embedding models in applications where robustness and alignment with human judgment are crucial. The identified trade-offs (e.g., clustering performance vs. robustness) are also valuable for model selection and application-specific fine-tuning.  The benchmark itself will likely become a valuable resource for researchers in the field, driving the development of more robust and reliable models. The finding that even current best models perform at 45.7% effectiveness under adversarial noise is a critical one.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The benchmark covers a wide range of aspects of semantic understanding.
    *   **Realistic Scenarios:**  The inclusion of adversarial conditions and noisy transformations makes the evaluation more relevant to real-world applications.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly describes the tasks, datasets, evaluation metrics, and implementation details.
    *   **Well-Documented Results:**  The results are presented in a clear and concise manner, with tables and examples highlighting the key findings.
    *   **Focus on Practical Implications:** The discussion section emphasizes the implications for practitioners and the need for more robust evaluation frameworks.
    *   **Diversity of Tasks:** Broadens the scope of evaluation beyond retrieval.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The benchmark's complexity might make it challenging for some researchers to adopt fully. Simplifying some of the transformation/noise application processes could encourage broader adoption.
    *   **Limited Scope of Models:** While nine embedding models were evaluated, a more extensive comparison across a wider range of models (including more specialized or domain-specific models) would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Potential for overfitting to the benchmark:** As with any benchmark, there's a risk that future models will be optimized specifically for SAGE, potentially diminishing its value over time. Addressing this would require ongoing updates and expansions to the benchmark.
    *   **Subjectivity of Human Preference:** Human preference alignment is inherently subjective, and the dataset used may not represent the full spectrum of human judgments. Future iterations could explore alternative sources of human feedback.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper and the SAGE benchmark have the potential to significantly impact the field of semantic understanding by:

    *   Driving the development of more robust and reliable models.
    *   Informing model selection and application-specific fine-tuning.
    *   Encouraging the development of new evaluation metrics and methodologies.
    *   Raising awareness of the limitations of current benchmarks and the importance of realistic evaluation.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty, significance, and potential impact of the SAGE benchmark, along with the rigorous evaluation presented in the paper, a score of 8.5 is warranted. While the paper has minor weaknesses related to the complexity and limited model scope, the strengths of the holistic benchmark, realistic evaluation scenarios, and practical implications outweigh these limitations. The paper fills a critical gap in the current landscape of semantic understanding evaluation and is likely to have a substantial influence on future research and development in the field.

**Score: 8.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### SD3.5-Flash: Distribution-Guided Distillation of Generative Flows
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21318v1)
- **Authors**: Hmrishav Bandyopadhyay, Rahim Entezari, Jim Scott, Reshinth Adithyan, Yi-Zhe Song, Varun Jampani
- **Abstract**: We present SD3.5-Flash, an efficient few-step distillation framework that brings high-quality image generation to accessible consumer devices. Our approach distills computationally prohibitive rectified flow models through a reformulated distribution matching objective tailored specifically for few-step generation. We introduce two key innovations: "timestep sharing" to reduce gradient noise and "split-timestep fine-tuning" to improve prompt alignment. Combined with comprehensive pipeline optimizations like text encoder restructuring and specialized quantization, our system enables both rapid generation and memory-efficient deployment across different hardware configurations. This democratizes access across the full spectrum of devices, from mobile phones to desktop computers. Through extensive evaluation including large-scale user studies, we demonstrate that SD3.5-Flash consistently outperforms existing few-step methods, making advanced generative AI truly accessible for practical deployment.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces SD3.5-Flash, a few-step distillation framework for generating high-quality images on consumer-grade devices. The core innovations are "timestep sharing," which improves gradient stability during training by leveraging trajectory samples, and "split-timestep fine-tuning," which addresses prompt alignment issues by temporarily expanding model capacity for different timestep ranges before merging. The paper also highlights pipeline optimizations, including text encoder restructuring and quantization techniques, that enable efficient deployment on various hardware configurations. Extensive user studies and evaluations demonstrate that SD3.5-Flash outperforms existing few-step methods while maintaining or even exceeding the quality of larger, slower models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper combines several ideas, some of which are inspired by existing work, but their specific combination and application within the context of rectified flow models for extreme few-step distillation demonstrates novelty. Timestep sharing is a key innovation for stabilizing gradients during training, and while the idea of splitting models for different tasks or timestep ranges isn't entirely new, its adaptation for prompt alignment is a distinct contribution.  The system-level optimizations (encoder dropout, quantization) are significant engineering contributions necessary for real-world deployment. The combination of these components to efficiently distill a model from high step rectified flows, for a low number of inference steps is a novel and well-executed contribution.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a significant barrier in generative AI: the computational cost of high-quality image generation. By enabling image generation on resource-constrained devices, SD3.5-Flash democratizes access to this technology.  The large-scale user studies and quantitative evaluations provide strong evidence of its practical value. The practical significance is further enhanced by its ability to maintain high quality at low VRAM usage. The comparison with other state-of-the-art few-step generation techniques in user studies is a crucial and significant contribution, demonstrating that the models are useful and preferred to existing ones in practice.

**Strengths:**

*   **Effective combination of techniques:** The paper synthesizes and adapts existing ideas in a novel and effective way to address the challenges of few-step distillation.
*   **Practical focus:** The paper emphasizes practical deployment and optimization, making it highly relevant for real-world applications.
*   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes extensive user studies, quantitative evaluations, and ablation studies to validate its effectiveness and demonstrate the importance of its components.
*   **Clear writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, making it accessible to a wide audience.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental novelty:** While the combination is novel, the individual components aren't entirely groundbreaking, with many techniques being adaptation and application of prior work.
*   **Limited theoretical depth:** While the empirical results are strong, the paper could benefit from a deeper theoretical analysis of the proposed techniques.
*   **Dependency on SD3.5:** The method is built on top of SD3.5, so its generalization to other foundation models remains an open question. However, that is a relatively minor drawback given the strong results.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field by making high-quality image generation more accessible. It also provides valuable insights into the challenges and opportunities of few-step distillation and efficient deployment.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** SD3.5-Flash presents a valuable contribution by addressing a critical challenge in generative AI: efficient and accessible high-quality image generation. While the individual components may not be entirely novel, the combination of "timestep sharing," "split-timestep fine-tuning," and system optimizations leads to a practically significant outcome. The user studies, quantitative metrics, and ablation studies support the claims made and demonstrate a notable improvement over existing few-step methods. Therefore, the work demonstrates genuine novelty and impact, however, is unlikely to be a paradigm-shifting contribution. For that reason, a score of 7.5 is justified.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SciReasoner: Laying the Scientific Reasoning Ground Across Disciplines
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21320v1)
- **Authors**: Yizhou Wang, Chen Tang, Han Deng, Jiabei Xiao, Jiaqi Liu, Jianyu Wu, Jun Yao, Pengze Li, Encheng Su, Lintao Wang, Guohang Zhuang, Yuchen Ren, Ben Fei, Ming Hu, Xin Chen, Dongzhan Zhou, Junjun He, Xiangyu Yue, Zhenfei Yin, Jiamin Wu, Qihao Zheng, Yuhao Zhou, Huihui Xu, Chenglong Ma, Yan Lu, Wenlong Zhang, Chunfeng Song, Philip Torr, Shixiang Tang, Xinzhu Ma, Wanli Ouyang, Lei Bai
- **Abstract**: We present a scientific reasoning foundation model that aligns natural language with heterogeneous scientific representations. The model is pretrained on a 206B-token corpus spanning scientific text, pure sequences, and sequence-text pairs, then aligned via SFT on 40M instructions, annealed cold-start bootstrapping to elicit long-form chain-of-thought, and reinforcement learning with task-specific reward shaping, which instills deliberate scientific reasoning. It supports four capability families, covering up to 103 tasks across workflows: (i) faithful translation between text and scientific formats, (ii) text/knowledge extraction, (iii) property prediction, (iv) property classification, (v) unconditional and conditional sequence generation and design. Compared with specialist systems, our approach broadens instruction coverage, improves cross-domain generalization, and enhances fidelity. We detail data curation and training and show that cross-discipline learning strengthens transfer and downstream reliability. The model, instruct tuning datasets and the evaluation code are open-sourced at https://huggingface.co/SciReason and https://github.com/open-sciencelab/SciReason.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SciReasoner: Laying the Scientific Reasoning Ground Across Disciplines":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SciReasoner, a foundation model designed for scientific reasoning. It aims to align natural language with heterogeneous scientific representations. The model is pre-trained on a large corpus of scientific text, sequences, and sequence-text pairs, then fine-tuned using supervised learning and reinforcement learning with task-specific reward shaping to enable deliberate scientific reasoning. SciReasoner supports capabilities spanning faithful translation between text and scientific formats, information extraction, property prediction and classification, and sequence generation/design. The authors demonstrate improvements in instruction coverage, cross-domain generalization, and fidelity compared to specialist systems. They also detail data curation/training procedures and show the positive effects of cross-discipline learning on transfer and reliability. Finally, the model, datasets, and evaluation code are open-sourced. The paper also proposes three key techniques: adaptive scientific reasoning which separates "instant" vs "thinking" tasks, task-grouped rewarding, and scientific reward softening.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core concept of building a large foundation model specifically for scientific reasoning, spanning multiple disciplines and modalities (text, sequences, structured data), is relatively novel. While other scientific LLMs exist, SciReasoner stands out due to its focus on deliberate reasoning and the integration of various scientific representations. The adaptive reasoning and task-grouped rewarding techniques provide further novelty. However, it builds upon existing techniques (e.g., reinforcement learning with reward shaping, multi-representation pretraining) but applies them in a novel scientific context.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in its potential to accelerate scientific discovery by providing a versatile tool for researchers. A unified model can bridge different scientific domains and automate tasks like data translation, information extraction, property prediction, and sequence generation, all of which contribute to faster research cycles. The open-source nature promotes reproducibility and further development by the community.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** Supports a wide range of scientific tasks (103).
    *   **Cross-Discipline Generalization:** Demonstrated improvements in generalization compared to specialized models.
    *   **Explicit Reasoning:** Focuses on inducing deliberate scientific reasoning through techniques like CoT generation and reward shaping.
    *   **Well-Defined Techniques**: Adaptive scientific reasoning, task grouped rewarding and reward softening.
    *   **Open-Source:** Promotes adoption, extension, and community involvement.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Evaluation Limitations:** The evaluation results, while promising, could benefit from more direct comparisons against state-of-the-art specialist systems on specific tasks (e.g., using exact match in some domains is overly strict). It would be helpful to better see how SciReasoner stacks up against the very best specialist systems on *their* tasks.
    *   **Complexity and Scale:** The model's scale (206B tokens) and training complexity may make it challenging for some researchers to replicate or fine-tune without significant resources, despite the open sourcing.
    *   **Dependence on Base Models:** The paper relies on top of Qwen models, potentially inheriting any limitations or biases from those base models.
    *   **Limited Analysis:** The results for some of the tasks require further analysis to clarify the trade-offs between the specialist model and the SciReason approach.

*   **Potential Influence:** SciReasoner could influence the field by:

    *   **Setting a new standard for general-purpose scientific LLMs:** It can encourage further research into cross-domain scientific reasoning and representation learning.
    *   **Empowering researchers:** Providing a single model that can perform a wide range of scientific tasks, potentially reducing the need for multiple specialized tools.
    *   **Facilitating collaboration and knowledge transfer:**  A shared foundation model can help researchers from different disciplines communicate and collaborate more effectively.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7/10**. The paper presents a significant step forward in creating a general-purpose scientific reasoning model. The comprehensive scope and demonstrated improvements in cross-domain generalization are compelling. However, the limited scope and direct comparisons to specialist models, dependency on the Qwen models, and high training resource requirements prevent it from scoring higher. The open-source nature and potential impact on scientific discovery, however, justify a positive assessment. The novelty primarily comes from the combination of techniques and focus on scientific reasoning rather than individual algorithmic breakthroughs. The main improvement stems from the cross-domain setting.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10
